## Area 7 — Layer L03: Emotional Expression Grammar

**Group:** I — Body and Senses · **Layer code:** `L03` · **Dimensions:** 9 · **Mandatory-core:** YES

**Governs:** the timing, channel and reliability with which this character's feeling reaches the surface — onset, decay, route, latency, substitution, leakage, and the two emotions that define them by being unfakeable and by being unmistakable.

**Boundary:** Part 26 owns what the character *feels* and at what graded intensity. Part 28 Area 7 owns the requirement that emotion arise from experience rather than assertion. L03 owns only the transfer function between the inner state and the observable surface. It never sets or revises a PIS level.

### 7.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.7.1.1 — The Transfer Function Is the Character.** Two people with identical grief are different people because one goes quiet and precise and the other becomes generous and talkative. Registering the grief is Part 26's work; registering that this person's grief arrives as method is L03's, and it is the half the reader actually experiences.

**Rule 39.7.1.2 — Asymmetry Requirement.** No character registers the same onset and decay profile for every emotion. Some emotions differ in their timing (two is the guide), and one takes a channel that surprises (one is the guide): the rage that arrives as courtesy, the fear that arrives as appetite, the love that arrives as criticism.

### 7.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L03-ONSET` — Speed of emotional arrival

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L03-ONSET-01` | Instant and total; the feeling is fully present before the event has finished happening |
| `B39-L03-ONSET-02` | Delayed by hours or a day; reacts correctly at the time and is felled later, alone |
| `B39-L03-ONSET-03` | Slow rise across a scene, so that the last line is at a pitch the first line did not predict |
| `B39-L03-ONSET-04` | Arrives on a physical cue rather than the event: sitting down, taking the coat off, reaching the door |
| `B39-L03-ONSET-05` | Arrives only when the responsibility ends, and not one moment before |
| `B39-L03-ONSET-06` | Arrives when another person names it, and not otherwise |
| `B39-L03-ONSET-07` | Different by valence: bad news instantly, good news slowly and with suspicion |
| `B39-L03-ONSET-08` | Arrives in the body first and is identified afterward, sometimes wrongly |
| `B39-L03-ONSET-09` | Suppressed at onset by trained reflex and surfaces at the next unrelated small failure |
| `B39-L03-ONSET-10` | Arrives in full and departs in full within a minute, which others mistake for shallowness |
| `B39-L03-ONSET-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L03-DECAY` — How long feeling persists

| Code | Option |
|---|---|

| `B39-L03-DECAY-02` | Never fully decays; every feeling is added to a standing accumulation |
| `B39-L03-DECAY-03` | Decays on completion of a task and cannot be dismissed any other way |
| `B39-L03-DECAY-04` | Decays only in a specific place, with a specific person, or on a specific object |
| `B39-L03-DECAY-05` | Decays overnight, entirely and reliably, whatever it was |
| `B39-L03-DECAY-06` | Anger decays fast and hurt decays slowly, and the two are constantly mistaken for each other |
| `B39-L03-DECAY-07` | Decays into its opposite: fury into tenderness, fear into recklessness |
| `B39-L03-DECAY-08` | Does not decay so much as get filed, retrievable intact years later at full strength |
| `B39-L03-DECAY-09` | Decays when spoken aloud, which is why they speak everything aloud |
| `B39-L03-DECAY-10` | Decays only after the person who caused it has been dealt with, one way or another |
| `B39-L03-DECAY-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L03-CHANNEL` — Where feeling goes in the body

| Code | Option |
|---|---|

| `B39-L03-CHANNEL-04` | Into motion; they must walk, and will walk out of a room mid-conversation to do it |
| `B39-L03-CHANNEL-05` | Into stillness so complete it is more alarming than shouting |
| `B39-L03-CHANNEL-06` | Into the jaw, throat and breath, where it is visible to anyone who knows to look |
| `B39-L03-CHANNEL-07` | Into work on an object: a blade cleaned, a book squared, a hearth rebuilt |
| `B39-L03-CHANNEL-08` | Into food, drink or its refusal |
| `B39-L03-CHANNEL-09` | Into cultivation practice, correctly or dangerously (Part 3 Qi Deviation interface) |
| `B39-L03-CHANNEL-10` | Into another person: they must be near someone, or must send everyone away |
| `B39-L03-CHANNEL-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L03-LATENCY` — Gap between feeling and showing

| Code | Option |
|---|---|

| `B39-L03-LATENCY-09` | No latency for anger, long latency for everything else |
| `B39-L03-LATENCY-10` | Latency is a trained professional skill and its failure is a registered event, not a mood |

| `B39-L03-MASK-09` | Joy presents as suspicion and a search for the flaw |
| `B39-L03-MASK-10` | No substitution; what is felt is what is shown, and this is itself remarkable in their world |

|---|---|

### 7.3 — Cross-References

Part 26 Areas 2–6 grade the inner event; L03 renders it. Part 28 Area 7 (Emotion-From-Experience Law) forbids asserted emotion; L03's channel and leak dimensions are the specification of what is shown instead. Part 18 Area 3.2's five villain psychological archetypes constrain L03-LATENCY and L03-MASK for villains (Standard N39-12).

## Area 8 — Layer L04: Body-State and Long-Term Condition

**Group:** I — Body and Senses · **Layer code:** `L04` · **Dimensions:** 7 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** behavior arising from the body over time rather than in the moment — chronic conditions, old wounds, weather sensitivity, illness, aging across a Phase, augmentation habit, and convalescence.

**Boundary:** Part 26 owns psychological injury; Part 27 owns the physical cascade of an injury event; Part 8 owns cybernetic and augmentation technology. L04 owns only the habitual conduct a long-standing body-state produces. It never invents an injury: every L04 registration traces to an established Part 27 physical consequence, an established Section 1 fact, or an author-specified pre-story condition.

### 8.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.8.1.1 — Condition Is Habit, Not Announcement.** A body-state is rendered by what the character does to accommodate it, never by narration reporting it. The reader learns about the shoulder because he changes hands to carry the case, not because the prose says the shoulder troubles him.

**Rule 39.8.1.2 — The Nine-Year Rule.** Phase 1 spans nine in-story years. A character above Tier C usually carries an L04 registration expected to change across that span, with the change registered in the Conditional Drift Map (Area 42) against an age, realm, or event trigger. A body that does not move across nine years reads as a prop; the report notes it as information.

### 8.2 — Dimension Catalog

|---|---|
| `B39-L04-CHRONIC-01` | None registered; the body is unremarkable and this is stated rather than left blank |

| `B39-L04-RECOVER-01` | Tests the limit daily and re-injures themselves at least once |
| `B39-L04-RECOVER-02` | Follows instruction exactly and resents every day of it aloud |
| `B39-L04-RECOVER-03` | Converts recovery into another kind of work: reading, planning, teaching from a chair |
| `B39-L04-RECOVER-04` | Cannot be idle and becomes genuinely unwell from the idleness itself |
| `B39-L04-RECOVER-05` | Uses the time deliberately and emerges with something nobody expected |
| `B39-L04-RECOVER-06` | Hides the extent of the injury so that recovery is never properly supervised |
| `B39-L04-RECOVER-07` | Accepts help gracefully in convalescence and never at any other time |
| `B39-L04-RECOVER-08` | Becomes superstitious or ritualistic about the recovery in a way that is new |
| `B39-L04-RECOVER-09` | Returns changed in one specific, permanent, registered way (Area 42 drift interface) |
| `B39-L04-RECOVER-10` | Refuses to acknowledge that recovery is a state at all and behaves as though already well |
| `B39-L04-RECOVER-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

### 8.3 — Cross-References

Part 27 Area 2 (physical cascades) is the source of any injury an L04 registration accommodates. Part 3 governs cultivation-based recovery rates and lifespan; Part 8 governs augmentation capability; Part 26 governs the psychological load of a permanent condition, which L04 does not duplicate.

## Area 9 — Layer L05: Consumption and Substance Behavior

**Group:** I — Body and Senses · **Layer code:** `L05` · **Dimensions:** 7 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** eating, drinking, stimulants, cultivation elixirs and their refusal, treated as behavior rather than logistics — including what a person consumes under pressure, the ritual around it, their discipline or its absence, and their conduct when impaired.

**Boundary:** Part 11 owns CPM substances, their grades and their physiological effects; Part 3 owns absorption physiology. L05 owns the habit, never the mechanism, and never grants access to a substance a character's realm or resources do not permit.

### 9.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.9.1.1 — Consumption Is Scene Furniture That Characterizes.** Meals, tea, water and rest are among the most frequent settings in any long narrative and among the most wasted. A registered L05 profile turns a shared meal into characterization without adding a word of exposition: who serves, who waits, who eats standing, who does not eat at all, and who notices.

### 9.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L05-FOOD` — How they eat

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L05-FOOD-01` | Fast, standing, and finished before others have sat, from years of eating between duties |
| `B39-L05-FOOD-02` | Slowly and completely, and will not be hurried by rank, emergency or company |
| `B39-L05-FOOD-03` | Eats what is in front of them without preference or comment, always |
| `B39-L05-FOOD-04` | Rearranges the plate before beginning, in a fixed order |
| `B39-L05-FOOD-05` | Serves everyone else first, including their own subordinates, before touching anything |
| `B39-L05-FOOD-06` | Talks through the meal and finishes last, food cold, having barely noticed |
| `B39-L05-FOOD-07` | Eats alone by preference and finds shared meals a duty |
| `B39-L05-FOOD-08` | Leaves something on the plate deliberately every time, for a registered reason |
| `B39-L05-FOOD-09` | Eats the same thing daily and is disturbed by variation |
| `B39-L05-FOOD-10` | Eats little and irregularly, from cultivation discipline or from indifference to the body |

| `B39-L05-DRINK-03` | Drinks alone and never socially, which nobody has yet noticed |
| `B39-L05-DRINK-04` | Holds a cup all evening and drinks almost nothing from it, on purpose |
| `B39-L05-DRINK-05` | Matches whoever they are with, drink for drink, as a form of courtesy or contest |
| `B39-L05-DRINK-06` | Keeps a specific stimulant — tea, leaf, powder, tonic — and their day is built around it |
| `B39-L05-DRINK-07` | Drinks heavily and functions perfectly, which is its own registered problem |
| `B39-L05-DRINK-08` | Uses drink deliberately as a tool on others and never on themselves |
| `B39-L05-DRINK-09` | Has one annual or occasional exception to an otherwise absolute rule |
| `B39-L05-DRINK-10` | Drinks only what they have made, brewed or verified themselves |

| `B39-L05-STRESS-05` | Feeds other people instead of themselves |
| `B39-L05-STRESS-06` | Drinks water compulsively and cannot say why |
| `B39-L05-STRESS-07` | Takes a stimulant they have been told to stop taking |
| `B39-L05-STRESS-08` | Consumption is unchanged by stress, and the invariance is a registered discipline |
| `B39-L05-STRESS-09` | Cannot swallow; the throat closes and they hide it by handling the cup |
| `B39-L05-STRESS-10` | Turns to cultivation-substance consumption in place of food, at a known cost (Part 11 interface) |
| `B39-L05-STRESS-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

| Code | Option |

# GROUP II — MIND (Layers L06–L09)

> **Group II governs the character as a thinking, deciding, concealing person.** L06 is how they decide, L07 is what the inside of their head sounds like on the page, L08 is what they do before they have decided anything, and L09 is what they do with the truth. Together they are the difference between a character who reaches the correct plot outcome and one whose reaching of it is interesting. L08 is mandatory-core at every tier.

---

## Area 10 — Layer L06: Cognitive and Decision Behavior

**Group:** II — Mind · **Layer code:** `L06` · **Dimensions:** 10 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** how this person arrives at a decision and what they do around it — speed, information appetite, risk posture, planning horizon, error response, attention allocation, memory habit, relationship to rules, time discipline, and what they do with an unresolvable choice.

**Boundary:** Part 20 owns what the character believes and therefore what they will decide; L06 owns how they get there. Two characters with identical ideologies decide differently, and that difference is L06's whole subject. Part 24 owns information as an asset; L06 owns the appetite for it.

### 10.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.10.1.1 — Decision Is Shown as Process, Not Verdict.** A character who is only ever shown announcing conclusions has no cognitive register. The registration is rendered in what they ask for first, what they refuse to decide without, how long they take, and what they do with the interval.

**Rule 39.10.1.2 — Registered Error.** Every character above Tier C registers `L06-ERROR` (how they respond to their own error) and `L36-SUBOPT` (the error they tend to make), and the error shows when the story calls for it: a character who makes many consequential decisions is expected, over the arc, to be wrong now and then in the way their registration predicts. No count of decisions requires an error, and none is added to meet one. A character whose decision process never produces a characteristic error has not been given a decision process, only a correctness (P39-13). The kind of error is the character's registered `L36-SUBOPT` value; `L06-ERROR` is how they respond to their own error.

### 10.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L06-SPEED` — Decision speed

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L06-SPEED-01` | Immediate, and revisited privately afterward at length |
| `B39-L06-SPEED-02` | Immediate, and never revisited at all |
| `B39-L06-SPEED-03` | Deliberately slow; makes the room wait and does not apologize for it |
| `B39-L06-SPEED-04` | Fast on small matters and paralyzingly slow on large ones |
| `B39-L06-SPEED-05` | Slow on small matters and instant on large ones, which unnerves people |
| `B39-L06-SPEED-06` | Will not decide until a stated condition is met, and states the condition |
| `B39-L06-SPEED-07` | Decides at the last possible moment by design, keeping options open past comfort |
| `B39-L06-SPEED-08` | Decides early and then spends the interval preparing for the decision to be wrong |
| `B39-L06-SPEED-09` | Speed scales inversely with how many people are watching |
| `B39-L06-SPEED-10` | Defers by default and decides only when nobody else will |
| `B39-L06-SPEED-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

|---|---|

| `B39-L06-INFO-03` | Wants the document, not the report; will not act on what was said, only on what was written |
| `B39-L06-INFO-04` | Wants the person, not the document; goes to see for themselves |
| `B39-L06-INFO-05` | Asks the least senior person present, on principle |

### 10.3 — Cross-References

Part 20 governs the values a decision serves; Part 24 governs the information available to it; Part 26 Area 10.2's four pre-combat states govern decision quality under combat load; Part 27 governs the consequences a decision incurs. L06 governs only the process.

## Area 11 — Layer L07: Interior Thought-Pattern Signature

**Group:** II — Mind · **Layer code:** `L07` · **Dimensions:** 9 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** what the inside of this character's head sounds like when the prose is in their POV — the mode of cognition, the grammar of self-address, rumination habit, mental counting, private formulae, what they rehearse, and the thought they will not permit themselves.

**Boundary:** Part 36 Area 8 (Character Interiority and Emotional Truth Mandate) requires interiority and sets its depth; Part 2 governs which POV is active. L07 owns the per-character texture of that interiority. A POV segment that satisfies Part 36's depth requirement in a voice that carries none of the character's registered L07 values has failed L07.

### 11.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.11.1.1 — The Head Test.** Strip the dialogue from a POV passage. Test the interior text that remains against this POV character's own L07 registration and against nothing else: it must still sound like their registered mind, that is, their mode of cognition, the grammar of self-address, what they count, what they rehearse and the thought they will not permit. If it carries none of these, L07 has not been applied. Interiority is not a quantity to be met; it is a particular mind to be occupied.

**Rule 39.11.1.2 — Non-POV Characters Still Register L07.** A character who never holds POV still registers this Layer. Their thought-pattern governs the observable evidence of thinking: what they say aloud by accident, what they mutter, what they write in a margin, and the pauses another POV reads as deliberation.

### 11.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L07-MODE` — Mode of cognition

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L07-MODE-01` | Verbal; thinks in sentences, and the sentences are close to what they would say aloud |
| `B39-L07-MODE-02` | Verbal but formal; thinks in a register more elevated than they ever speak |
| `B39-L07-MODE-03` | Image-led; thinks in pictures and translates to words only at the point of speech |
| `B39-L07-MODE-04` | Spatial; thinks in positions, distances and diagrams |
| `B39-L07-MODE-05` | Numeric; quantities arrive before qualities |
| `B39-L07-MODE-06` | Procedural; thinks in ordered steps and is uncomfortable outside a sequence |
| `B39-L07-MODE-07` | Comparative; every new thing is understood as like or unlike a remembered thing |
| `B39-L07-MODE-08` | Physical; understands by imagining the doing of it, hands first |
| `B39-L07-MODE-09` | Dialogic; thinks by arguing with an absent person |
| `B39-L07-MODE-10` | Somatic; the body reaches conclusions the mind then has to catch up with |

|---|---|

| `B39-L07-SELF-08` | Speaks to the object, animal or instrument in front of them rather than to themselves |
| `B39-L07-SELF-09` | Addresses a deity, ancestor, or cultivation principle in a fixed formula |

| `B39-L07-RUMIN-06` | Ruminates on other people's failures rather than their own |

## Area 12 — Layer L08: Moral Reflex and Conscience Behavior

**Group:** II — Mind · **Layer code:** `L08` · **Dimensions:** 8 · **Mandatory-core:** YES

**Governs:** what this person does before they have thought — the pre-deliberative moral act, the line they will not cross, conduct when unobserved, treatment of the powerless, response to an unearned advantage, the shape of their guilt, what they will pay to be clean, and the gap between the ethic they state and the one they perform.

**Boundary:** Part 20 owns ideology — the organized belief system, its categories, its polarity and its events. L08 owns the reflex that precedes belief and frequently contradicts it. The distinction is load-bearing: Part 20's Five Ideological Hypocrisy types describe the *inconsistency*; L08 registers the *specific reflex* that produces it in this person. L08 never assigns or alters an ideology category.

### 12.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.12.1.1 — The Reflex Precedes the Argument.** A moral reflex is rendered as an action taken before the character has justified it, and often before the reader knows why. The justification, if it comes, comes after, and may be wrong about its own cause.

**Rule 39.12.1.2 — Unobserved Conduct.** A character above Tier C is usually shown, at some point in their arc, behaving when they believe themselves unobserved. This is a very efficient characterizing beat; the confirmed Chapter Spec decides when and whether it is used.

### 12.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L08-REFLEX` — The pre-deliberative act

|---|---|

### 12.3 — Cross-References

Part 20 Areas 1 and 5 (Moral Orientations; Hypocrisy types) own the belief architecture L08 sits beneath; Part 26 governs moral injury as a psychological event; Part 27 governs the consequences a moral act incurs; Part 18 Area 3.2's archetypes constrain villain L08-LINE and L08-GAP registrations.

## Area 13 — Layer L09: Deception and Concealment Behavior

**Group:** II — Mind · **Layer code:** `L09` · **Dimensions:** 8 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** what this person does with the truth — whether they can lie, the mechanism they use, their tell, what they conceal by default, how they hold a secret in company, their conduct when caught, their default disclosure posture, and what they do with someone else's secret.

**Boundary:** Part 24 owns information as an asset — its acquisition, value, transmission and strategic use. Part 27 owns betrayal as a consequence event. L09 owns the personal mechanics of concealment. A registered L09 value never overrides a Part 24 information state: a character cannot conceal what the information ledger says they do not know.

### 13.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.13.1.1 — The Tell Is For the Reader, Not the Room.** A registered deception tell is rendered so that the reader can learn it across scenes, whether or not any character in the room ever notices. A tell that is always caught is a plot device; a tell that is never shown is not registered.

**Rule 39.13.1.2 — Honest Characters Register This Layer Too.** A character who cannot or will not lie registers `L09-CAPACITY` accordingly, and that registration does work: it governs how they behave when the truth is unspeakable, which is one of the most reliable sources of pressure available.

### 13.2 — Dimension Catalog

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L09-CAPACITY-01` | Cannot lie outright; will go silent, leave, or answer a different question instead |

| `B39-L09-CAPACITY-05` | Lies only by omission and holds that this is not lying |
| `B39-L09-CAPACITY-06` | Lies only in a professional register and not personally, with the line absolute |
| `B39-L09-CAPACITY-07` | Lies only to protect a third party and cannot do it for their own benefit |
| `B39-L09-CAPACITY-08` | Will lie to anyone except one named person |
| `B39-L09-CAPACITY-09` | Refuses on principle, states the refusal, and accepts the cost publicly |
| `B39-L09-CAPACITY-10` | Believes their own lies within a short interval, which makes them unusually convincing |

|---|---|
| `B39-L09-HOLD-01` | Behaves normally and the secret leaves no trace on their conduct |
| `B39-L09-HOLD-02` | Becomes quieter; the withheld thing costs them speech |
| `B39-L09-HOLD-03` | Becomes more talkative on unrelated subjects |
| `B39-L09-HOLD-04` | Cannot be near the person it concerns, and arranges not to be |
| `B39-L09-HOLD-05` | Tests the water repeatedly with adjacent questions |
| `B39-L09-HOLD-06` | Writes it down somewhere, which is a registered vulnerability |
| `B39-L09-HOLD-07` | Tells exactly one person and considers that within the terms of holding it |
| `B39-L09-HOLD-08` | Holds it comfortably for years and is genuinely unbothered |
| `B39-L09-HOLD-09` | Holds it by converting it into a procedure: as long as the routine runs, it stays down |
| `B39-L09-HOLD-10` | Cannot hold it; will disclose within a predictable interval, and knows this about themselves |
| `B39-L09-HOLD-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L09-CAUGHT` — Conduct when caught

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L09-CAUGHT-01` | Concedes immediately and completely, and does not attempt a second position |
| `B39-L09-CAUGHT-02` | Concedes the caught portion and defends the rest |
| `B39-L09-CAUGHT-03` | Denies past all evidence and forces the other party to prove it |
| `B39-L09-CAUGHT-04` | Attacks the enquiry rather than answering it |
| `B39-L09-CAUGHT-05` | Explains the reason at length, treating justification as equivalent to admission |
| `B39-L09-CAUGHT-06` | Goes silent and offers nothing further, whatever is asked |
| `B39-L09-CAUGHT-07` | Offers restitution before being asked for it |
| `B39-L09-CAUGHT-08` | Laughs, genuinely, and the laugh is the whole response |
| `B39-L09-CAUGHT-09` | Turns it into a lesson directed at whoever caught them |
| `B39-L09-CAUGHT-10` | Never has been caught; the state is unknown and the registration says so |
| `B39-L09-CAUGHT-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L09-DISCLOSE` — Default disclosure posture

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L09-DISCLOSE-01` | Tells everything to everyone who has a role in it, immediately |
| `B39-L09-DISCLOSE-02` | Tells what is needed, when it is needed, and no earlier |
| `B39-L09-DISCLOSE-03` | Tells upward fully and downward minimally |
| `B39-L09-DISCLOSE-04` | Tells downward fully and upward minimally, which is a career risk they accept |
| `B39-L09-DISCLOSE-05` | Compartments everything by default; no one person holds the whole picture |
| `B39-L09-DISCLOSE-06` | Discloses in writing only, so that the disclosure is provable |
| `B39-L09-DISCLOSE-07` | Discloses verbally only, so that it is not |
| `B39-L09-DISCLOSE-08` | Trades disclosure; information is currency and is priced |
| `B39-L09-DISCLOSE-09` | Discloses to a fixed named confidant regardless of relevance |
| `B39-L09-DISCLOSE-10` | Discloses late and then blames the delay on procedure |
| `B39-L09-DISCLOSE-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L09-OTHERS` — Handling someone else's secret

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L09-OTHERS-01` | Keeps it absolutely, and would be destroyed rather than break it |
| `B39-L09-OTHERS-02` | Keeps it until it conflicts with duty, and duty wins with visible cost |
| `B39-L09-OTHERS-03` | Keeps it until it conflicts with duty, and the secret wins |
| `B39-L09-OTHERS-04` | Does not want it; actively refuses to be told things |
| `B39-L09-OTHERS-05` | Collects them, without using them, as a matter of temperament |
| `B39-L09-OTHERS-06` | Uses them, as leverage, and considers this ordinary practice |
| `B39-L09-OTHERS-07` | Tells the subject that they know, immediately, as a courtesy |
| `B39-L09-OTHERS-08` | Passes it upward as a matter of procedure and does not regard it as betrayal |
| `B39-L09-OTHERS-09` | Cannot hold it and always tells exactly one further person |
| `B39-L09-OTHERS-10` | Acts on it without disclosing it, arranging outcomes the subject cannot account for |
| `B39-L09-OTHERS-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

### 13.3 — Cross-References

Part 24 owns the information state a concealment operates on; Part 27 Area 8 owns betrayal as a consequence cascade; Part 29 Area 11 (Subtext and the Unsaid) governs how concealment renders in dialogue; Part 20 governs whether a given deception is ideologically permissible to this character.

---

# GROUP III — SPEECH AND EXCHANGE (Layers L10–L12)

> **Group III governs everything about talking that Part 29 Area 4.4 does not own.** The nine prosodic determinants govern how a character builds a sentence. Group III governs how they occupy a conversation: when they take a turn, what they do with silence, how they refuse, how they apologize, whether they joke, and how they deal. Rule 39.2.3.1 (No Prosodic Re-Registration) is absolutely binding across this Group. L10 is mandatory-core at every tier.

---

## Area 14 — Layer L10: Speech-Adjacent Behavior

**Group:** III — Speech and Exchange · **Layer code:** `L10` · **Dimensions:** 11 · **Mandatory-core:** YES

**Governs:** turn-taking, silence, listening conduct, interruption and its tolerance, refusal form, apology form, praise and criticism delivery, naming habit, oath and expletive style, what they say when they have nothing to say, and how they end an exchange.

**Boundary — restated because this Layer is the highest collision risk in Part 39.** Part 29 Rule 29.4.4.1's nine determinants — sentence-length distribution, fragment tolerance, subordination depth, question habit, hedging versus bluntness, epigram appetite, technical-register default, pause and trail-off profile, figures-first ordering — are Part 29's exclusively. L10 registers no value that reduces to any of them. A registration reading *speaks in short sentences under pressure* is determinant 1 and is rejected here; *stops speaking entirely and lets the other person fill the gap* is turn-taking behavior and is accepted. An option that names a deliberate act or device is conduct and belongs here, even where the act shows in how a sentence is built (Rule 39.2.3.1).

### 14.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.14.1.1 — Conversation Is Occupied, Not Just Spoken.** A scene with four speakers has four people doing something during the three-quarters of it in which they are not talking. L10 registers what that something is. A drafted scene in which non-speaking characters simply wait for their line has failed this Layer whether or not the dialogue itself is compliant.

**Rule 39.14.1.2 — The Friction Floor Interface.** Part 29 Rule 29.4.4.4 looks for an authentic-imperfection beat per dialogue scene as a guide. L10-INTERRUPT, L10-SILENCE and L10-FILLER are the registered per-character sources of that beat. The Friction Floor tells the scene it needs friction; L10 tells it whose friction, and of what kind. Neither the em-dash prohibition (Rule 29.2.1.1b) nor the contraction rule (Rule 29.2.1.1c) is touched by any L10 registration. An L10 registration is a trait delivered in natural speech (Part 29 Rules 29.2.1.7 and 29.2.1.7a, Canon Amendments 2026-10-08 and 2026-10-16), never a sentence formula.

### 14.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L10-TURN` — Turn-taking

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L10-TURN-01` | Waits for a full stop and a beat beyond it before beginning |
| `B39-L10-TURN-02` | Takes the turn the moment the sense is complete, before the sentence is |
| `B39-L10-TURN-03` | Speaks only when addressed directly, and this holds even under provocation |
| `B39-L10-TURN-04` | Announces the turn with a physical act — a paper turned, a cup set down, a step forward |
| `B39-L10-TURN-05` | Takes the last turn in any exchange, as a matter of habit rather than of victory |
| `B39-L10-TURN-06` | Takes the first turn, always, and sets the frame before anyone else can |
| `B39-L10-TURN-07` | Cedes the turn readily and reclaims it later with the original point intact |
| `B39-L10-TURN-08` | Speaks over others without noticing they were speaking |
| `B39-L10-TURN-09` | Turn-taking is strictly by rank, and they enforce the order on others as well |
| `B39-L10-TURN-10` | Waits until the room has said everything and then speaks once |
| `B39-L10-TURN-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

|---|---|

| `B39-L10-SILENCE-02` | Cannot tolerate it and fills every gap, including gaps that were doing work |
| `B39-L10-SILENCE-03` | Fills it with an act rather than a word |
| `B39-L10-SILENCE-04` | Treats silence as agreement and proceeds accordingly, frequently wrongly |
| `B39-L10-SILENCE-05` | Treats silence as refusal and escalates into it |
| `B39-L10-SILENCE-06` | Answers a question with silence when the answer would be a lie |
| `B39-L10-SILENCE-07` | Silence is their refusal form and everyone who works with them knows it |
| `B39-L10-SILENCE-08` | Never silent in company and entirely silent alone |
| `B39-L10-SILENCE-09` | Uses silence only downward in rank, never upward |
| `B39-L10-SILENCE-10` | Silence is a courtesy they extend to let someone else reach a conclusion unaided |

#### `L10-LISTEN` — What they do while listening

| Code | Option |
|---|---|

| `B39-L10-LISTEN-02` | Continues working and hears everything, and proves it later |
| `B39-L10-LISTEN-03` | Continues working and hears nothing, and does not know the difference |
| `B39-L10-LISTEN-04` | Writes; the listening is a transcription and the pen is the tell for what mattered |
| `B39-L10-LISTEN-05` | Nods on a fixed rhythm unrelated to content |
| `B39-L10-LISTEN-06` | Watches a third party's reaction rather than the speaker |
| `B39-L10-LISTEN-07` | Visibly composes their reply and stops listening at the point they have it |
| `B39-L10-LISTEN-08` | Repeats the last clause back before responding, as a verification habit |
| `B39-L10-LISTEN-09` | Closes their eyes, which is either concentration or dismissal and is never clarified |
| `B39-L10-LISTEN-10` | Moves closer, physically, as the speaker approaches the real point |

### 14.3 — Cross-References

Part 29 Area 4.4 owns prosody and is not duplicated here; Part 29 Area 8 governs multi-party conversation structure; Part 29 Area 14 governs the meaning of non-speech social acts; Part 29 Area 16 owns address terms; Part 29 Rule 29.4.4.4 draws its per-character friction from this Layer.

## Area 15 — Layer L11: Humor and Play Behavior

**Group:** III — Speech and Exchange · **Layer code:** `L11` · **Dimensions:** 7 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** whether and how this person is funny, who they will joke with, how they receive a joke, whether they can be teased, what their humor conceals, and their conduct in games, contests and play.

**Boundary:** Part 29 Area 3's twelve dialogue content registers include humor as a register; L11 owns the per-character mechanism and its social rules. Part 36 Area 12's tone registers govern the scene's tone; a character's registered humor does not license a tonal breach of the scene.

### 15.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.15.1.1 — Humorlessness Is a Registration, Not an Omission.** A character who never jokes registers `L11-MECH-10` and the registration points to a beat the prose may use: the prose may show them failing to catch a joke, or receiving one with visible incomprehension, when the story offers the moment. Absence of humor that is simply never addressed is an unaddressed dimension.

**Rule 39.15.1.2 — Humor Is Relational.** An L11 registration usually names a person the character will joke with and one they never will. A character who is equally funny with everyone has a joke style, not a humor behavior. The two names go in the value text after the code (Rule 39.3.2.3); `B39-L11-WITH-07` is the one exemption and registers no never-person.

### 15.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L11-MECH` — Humor mechanism

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L11-MECH-01` | Dry understatement, delivered flat, and frequently missed |
| `B39-L11-MECH-02` | Exaggeration to the point of absurdity, built up across several turns |
| `B39-L11-MECH-03` | Mockery of the powerful, precisely aimed, and never of the weak |
| `B39-L11-MECH-04` | Mockery of themselves, deployed to defuse attention |

| `B39-L11-MECH-06` | Physical: the joke is an act, a mimicry, or an object placed somewhere |
| `B39-L11-MECH-07` | Trade humor, incomprehensible outside their craft and very funny inside it |
| `B39-L11-MECH-08` | Deadpan false statement, maintained until the other person breaks |
| `B39-L11-MECH-09` | Gallows humor, specifically about the danger currently present |
| `B39-L11-MECH-10` | None; humor is not a mode available to them, and its absence is characteristic |
| `B39-L11-MECH-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

|---|---|

| `B39-L11-CONCEAL-07` | Exhaustion; the jokes get worse as they get more tired |
| `B39-L11-CONCEAL-08` | A test of whoever is listening |
| `B39-L11-CONCEAL-09` | Their own uncertainty about whether they belong in the room |
| `B39-L11-CONCEAL-10` | Cruelty; the humor is the delivery system and this is a registered flaw |
| `B39-L11-CONCEAL-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L11-GAME` — Conduct in games and contests

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L11-GAME-01` | Plays to win absolutely, in every context, including with children |
| `B39-L11-GAME-02` | Plays to win and conceals it under apparent indifference |
| `B39-L11-GAME-03` | Loses deliberately to specific people and is caught at it |
| `B39-L11-GAME-04` | Refuses to play; regards games as a waste and says so |
| `B39-L11-GAME-05` | Plays badly and enjoys it, which others find disarming |
| `B39-L11-GAME-06` | Treats every game as training and cannot play for its own sake |
| `B39-L11-GAME-07` | Cheats in small ways, openly, as part of the game |
| `B39-L11-GAME-08` | Learns the opponent rather than the game |
| `B39-L11-GAME-09` | Plays only games of their own culture and refuses foreign ones |
| `B39-L11-GAME-10` | Cannot lose gracefully, and this is a known and costly limitation |
| `B39-L11-GAME-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L11-PLAY` — Play with children and animals

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L11-PLAY-01` | Plays fully and without self-consciousness, in front of anyone |
| `B39-L11-PLAY-02` | Plays only when unobserved by adults |
| `B39-L11-PLAY-03` | Converts play into instruction within a few minutes, every time |
| `B39-L11-PLAY-04` | Does not play; addresses children as small adults, which some children prefer |
| `B39-L11-PLAY-05` | Is awkward at it and keeps trying, which is itself endearing |
| `B39-L11-PLAY-06` | Plays roughly, and has to be told to stop |
| `B39-L11-PLAY-07` | Has one game they know and play it every time |
| `B39-L11-PLAY-08` | Plays with animals and not with children, or the exact reverse |
| `B39-L11-PLAY-09` | Uses play to gather information from a child, deliberately |
| `B39-L11-PLAY-10` | Not applicable; character is a child (the child's play register is recorded as a `-99` value under `L30-ANCHOR`) |

### 15.3 — Cross-References

Part 29 Area 3 (Dialogue Content Law) governs humor as a content register; Part 25 governs which humor is culturally legible; Part 36 Area 12 governs the scene's tonal register; Layer L16 governs the caregiving conduct L11-PLAY sits beside.

## Area 16 — Layer L12: Negotiation and Transaction Behavior

**Group:** III — Speech and Exchange · **Layer code:** `L12` · **Dimensions:** 8 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** how this person deals — opening posture, concession pattern, what they will not trade, conduct when out-leveraged, gift and bribe posture, treatment of the other side's subordinates, closing behavior, and how they behave after the deal is struck.

**Boundary:** Part 23 owns affiliation agreements and their binding force; Part 24 owns information trade; Part 22 owns faction-level bargaining authority. L12 owns the personal conduct of a negotiation. A registered L12 behavior never grants a character authority to bind a faction that Part 22 does not give them.

### 16.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.16.1.1 — The Deal Is a Scene, Not a Summary.** Where a negotiation matters, it is rendered as conduct: who sits, who speaks first, what is put on the table physically, who fills the silence. A negotiation summarized in narration has spent the scene and gained no characterization.

### 16.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L12-OPEN` — Opening posture

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L12-OPEN-01` | States their real position immediately and does not move from it |
| `B39-L12-OPEN-02` | Opens far past what they want and expects the ritual of retreat |
| `B39-L12-OPEN-03` | Refuses to open; makes the other side speak first, every time |
| `B39-L12-OPEN-04` | Opens with what the other side needs rather than what they want |
| `B39-L12-OPEN-05` | Opens with the constraint: states what is impossible before what is possible |
| `B39-L12-OPEN-06` | Opens with a gift, a courtesy, or a concession given free |
| `B39-L12-OPEN-07` | Opens with the record: what was agreed before, and by whom |
| `B39-L12-OPEN-08` | Opens by establishing the deadline |
| `B39-L12-OPEN-09` | Opens socially and will not discuss the matter for a stated interval |
| `B39-L12-OPEN-10` | Opens by naming what they think the other side is really after |
| `B39-L12-OPEN-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L12-CONCEDE` — Concession pattern

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L12-CONCEDE-01` | Concedes in equal measured steps and stops dead at a pre-set floor |
| `B39-L12-CONCEDE-02` | Concedes nothing until the end and then concedes everything at once |
| `B39-L12-CONCEDE-03` | Concedes early and generously to establish goodwill, and is often exploited |
| `B39-L12-CONCEDE-04` | Trades concession for concession, strictly, and will not move unreciprocated |
| `B39-L12-CONCEDE-05` | Concedes on substance and holds absolutely on form, or the reverse |
| `B39-L12-CONCEDE-06` | Concedes only what costs them nothing and presents it as costly |
| `B39-L12-CONCEDE-07` | Withdraws a concession already made when the other side over-reaches |
| `B39-L12-CONCEDE-08` | Cannot concede in person; needs a night, a walk, or an intermediary |
| `B39-L12-CONCEDE-09` | Concedes to end the discomfort of the disagreement itself |
| `B39-L12-CONCEDE-10` | Never concedes; the deal either matches their terms or does not happen |
| `B39-L12-CONCEDE-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L12-NOTRADE` — What is not for trade

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L12-NOTRADE-01` | People; will not include a person as a term of any agreement |
| `B39-L12-NOTRADE-02` | The record; will not agree to alter, omit or destroy a document |
| `B39-L12-NOTRADE-03` | Time; will not agree to a schedule they do not believe can be met |
| `B39-L12-NOTRADE-04` | Their own word, once given, at any price |
| `B39-L12-NOTRADE-05` | The safety margin; will not trade away the reserve, whatever is offered for it |
| `B39-L12-NOTRADE-06` | Anything belonging to a subordinate who is not in the room |
| `B39-L12-NOTRADE-07` | A craft standard; will not agree to build, write or certify below it |
| `B39-L12-NOTRADE-08` | Precedent; will not agree to a term that would bind future cases badly |
| `B39-L12-NOTRADE-09` | Nothing is off the table, and they say so at the outset |
| `B39-L12-NOTRADE-10` | One named object, place or person, for a registered personal reason |
| `B39-L12-NOTRADE-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L12-WEAK` — Conduct when out-leveraged

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L12-WEAK-01` | Names their weakness aloud and negotiates from it openly |
| `B39-L12-WEAK-02` | Conceals it and bluffs, competently |
| `B39-L12-WEAK-03` | Conceals it and bluffs, badly, and is read |
| `B39-L12-WEAK-04` | Walks away rather than deal from weakness, whatever the cost |
| `B39-L12-WEAK-05` | Appeals to the other party's honor or self-image |
| `B39-L12-WEAK-06` | Widens the frame: brings in a third interest that changes the arithmetic |
| `B39-L12-WEAK-07` | Delays; buys time as the only available currency |
| `B39-L12-WEAK-08` | Accepts the bad terms fully and immediately, and begins planning the reversal |
| `B39-L12-WEAK-09` | Escalates to a superior and accepts the cost of having done so |
| `B39-L12-WEAK-10` | Becomes markedly more courteous, which is their pressure response |
| `B39-L12-WEAK-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L12-GIFT` — Gift and inducement posture

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L12-GIFT-01` | Refuses all gifts of any value, and the rule is publicly known |
| `B39-L12-GIFT-02` | Accepts gifts and records them, openly, in a register anyone may read |
| `B39-L12-GIFT-03` | Accepts and reciprocates immediately at equal value, to close the obligation |
| `B39-L12-GIFT-04` | Gives freely and refuses to receive |
| `B39-L12-GIFT-05` | Accepts hospitality but never objects |
| `B39-L12-GIFT-06` | Uses inducement routinely and regards it as ordinary practice |
| `B39-L12-GIFT-07` | Accepts on behalf of the institution and never personally |
| `B39-L12-GIFT-08` | Gives a specific customary gift on a fixed occasion and nothing at other times |
| `B39-L12-GIFT-09` | Accepts only from those with nothing to gain, which is their whole test |
| `B39-L12-GIFT-10` | Takes the gift and does the opposite, deliberately |
| `B39-L12-GIFT-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L12-SUBORD` — Treatment of the other side's subordinates

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L12-SUBORD-01` | Addresses them directly and by name, which the principals dislike |
| `B39-L12-SUBORD-02` | Ignores them entirely as a matter of protocol |
| `B39-L12-SUBORD-03` | Works them; gathers from them what the principal will not say |
| `B39-L12-SUBORD-04` | Protects them from their own principal's displeasure, visibly |
| `B39-L12-SUBORD-05` | Treats them exactly as their own, which reads as either respect or presumption |
| `B39-L12-SUBORD-06` | Is courteous to them and cold to the principal |
| `B39-L12-SUBORD-07` | Recruits them, or tries to, in the middle of the negotiation |
| `B39-L12-SUBORD-08` | Notices which of them the principal actually listens to and speaks to that one |
| `B39-L12-SUBORD-09` | Uses them to deliver what they will not say to the principal directly |
| `B39-L12-SUBORD-10` | Is harsh with them as a pressure tactic on the principal |
| `B39-L12-SUBORD-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L12-CLOSE` — Closing behavior

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L12-CLOSE-01` | Wants it written and sealed before anyone leaves the room |
| `B39-L12-CLOSE-02` | Takes a handshake or a word and considers paper an insult |
| `B39-L12-CLOSE-03` | Restates the whole agreement aloud before closing, item by item |
| `B39-L12-CLOSE-04` | Adds one small final term at the moment of closing, every time |
| `B39-L12-CLOSE-05` | Leaves one term deliberately unresolved for later leverage |
| `B39-L12-CLOSE-06` | Insists on a witness, always |
| `B39-L12-CLOSE-07` | Closes and immediately begins performing their side, before the ink dries |
| `B39-L12-CLOSE-08` | Cannot close; circles the agreement and needs another party to call it done |
| `B39-L12-CLOSE-09` | Marks the close with a fixed ritual: a drink, a token, a formula |
| `B39-L12-CLOSE-10` | Closes coldly and leaves at once, which the other side reads as displeasure |
| `B39-L12-CLOSE-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L12-AFTER` — Conduct after the deal

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L12-AFTER-01` | Performs exactly to the terms and not one item beyond |
| `B39-L12-AFTER-02` | Performs generously beyond the terms as a matter of course |
| `B39-L12-AFTER-03` | Audits the other side's performance closely and raises every shortfall |
| `B39-L12-AFTER-04` | Never mentions the agreement again and is offended when others do |
| `B39-L12-AFTER-05` | Re-opens terms whenever circumstances shift |
| `B39-L12-AFTER-06` | Regards a concluded deal as the start of a relationship rather than the end of a matter |
| `B39-L12-AFTER-07` | Second-guesses their own terms afterward, privately and at length |
| `B39-L12-AFTER-08` | Tells the story of the negotiation afterward, and improves it |
| `B39-L12-AFTER-09` | Keeps a record of every deal and their outcome, and consults it before the next |
| `B39-L12-AFTER-10` | Performs the terms and quietly withholds the goodwill, permanently |
| `B39-L12-AFTER-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

### 16.3 — Cross-References

Part 23 owns affiliation agreements and their breach; Part 22 owns who may bind a faction; Part 24 owns information as consideration; Part 27 Areas 4.2 and 4.4 own the consequence of a broken promise or agreement; Layer L18 owns the oath that a formal agreement may invoke.

---

# GROUP IV — PEOPLE (Layers L13–L18)

> **Group IV governs the character among other people.** L13 is their general social conduct; L14 is the per-pair override table that lets the same person be different with their father, their rival and their clerk without being inconsistent; L15 is authority performed; L16 is care given; L17 is intimacy; L18 is the binding word. These six Layers between them carry most of what a reader means when they say a cast feels inhabited.

---

## Area 17 — Layer L13: Social and Interaction Behavior

**Group:** IV — People · **Layer code:** `L13` · **Dimensions:** 11 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** general conduct with people — stance toward strangers, behavior upward and downward in hierarchy, trust rate, conflict style, role taken in a group, reciprocity and debt, boundary defence, the public-private divergence, loyalty expression, response to another's betrayal, and how they behave when they are the outsider.

**Boundary:** Part 29 Area 5 owns how power is expressed in speech; Part 23 owns formal affiliation bonds; Part 21/22 own the social structures within which these behaviors occur. L13 owns the personal conduct. Where a registered L13 behavior conflicts with a Part 29 Area 16 locked address standard, the address standard governs and L13 supplies the surrounding conduct only.

### 17.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.17.1.1 — The Mask Is Shown Both Sides.** A character registering a public-private divergence (`L13-MASK`) is usually shown in both registers across their arc; the confirmed Chapter Spec decides when. A mask asserted and never lifted is a claim, not a characterization.

**Rule 39.17.1.2 — Rank Behavior Is Rendered Physically First.** Deference and authority reach the page as position, timing and object handling before they reach it as diction. Who stands, who is seated, who waits, who is served, who touches the document first — these carry the hierarchy, and the speech follows.

### 17.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L13-STRANGER` — Default stance toward strangers

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L13-STRANGER-01` | Open and immediately conversational, with no assessment period at all |
| `B39-L13-STRANGER-02` | Correct and closed; full courtesy, zero disclosure, until they have a reason |
| `B39-L13-STRANGER-03` | Assesses threat first and everything else after |
| `B39-L13-STRANGER-04` | Assesses usefulness first, and it shows |
| `B39-L13-STRANGER-05` | Assumes competence until disproved |
| `B39-L13-STRANGER-06` | Assumes incompetence until proved otherwise, and makes people earn the correction |
| `B39-L13-STRANGER-07` | Defers by default until rank is established, then adjusts sharply |
| `B39-L13-STRANGER-08` | Tests them with a small request or a small trust |
| `B39-L13-STRANGER-09` | Names their own position and business first, always, as an opening offer |
| `B39-L13-STRANGER-10` | Avoids strangers entirely where possible and delegates the encounter |
| `B39-L13-STRANGER-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L13-UP` — Behavior toward superiors

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L13-UP-01` | Formally correct and substantively immovable; the courtesy conceals no yielding |
| `B39-L13-UP-02` | Anticipates: arrives with the thing already done |
| `B39-L13-UP-03` | Reports problems immediately, including their own, and does not soften them |
| `B39-L13-UP-04` | Reports only solutions and conceals the problems that had them |
| `B39-L13-UP-05` | Disagrees only in private, and always in private, without exception |
| `B39-L13-UP-06` | Disagrees in public, once, and then complies fully |
| `B39-L13-UP-07` | Physically deferent past what the rank requires, from long conditioning |
| `B39-L13-UP-08` | Treats superiors as peers and has been warned about it |
| `B39-L13-UP-09` | Manages upward deliberately, shaping what reaches the superior |
| `B39-L13-UP-10` | Loyal to the office and openly indifferent to whoever currently holds it |
| `B39-L13-UP-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L13-DOWN` — Behavior toward subordinates

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L13-DOWN-01` | Explains the reason for every order, always, however pressed for time |
| `B39-L13-DOWN-02` | Explains nothing and expects execution |
| `B39-L13-DOWN-03` | Takes the worst share of the work themselves, visibly |
| `B39-L13-DOWN-04` | Protects them upward absolutely and corrects them downward severely |
| `B39-L13-DOWN-05` | Knows their circumstances — families, injuries, debts — and acts on that knowledge |
| `B39-L13-DOWN-06` | Maintains distance deliberately, believing familiarity costs lives |
| `B39-L13-DOWN-07` | Has favourites and does not conceal it |
| `B39-L13-DOWN-08` | Delegates fully and does not check |
| `B39-L13-DOWN-09` | Delegates and then does it again themselves, which is corrosive and known |
| `B39-L13-DOWN-10` | Treats subordinates as future superiors and trains accordingly |
| `B39-L13-DOWN-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L13-TRUST` — Trust-building rate

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L13-TRUST-01` | Immediate and total, and repeatedly punished for it without adjusting |
| `B39-L13-TRUST-02` | Immediate and total, and withdrawn permanently at the first breach |
| `B39-L13-TRUST-03` | Slow, incremental, and tested at each step with a rising stake |
| `B39-L13-TRUST-04` | Trusts competence quickly and character never |
| `B39-L13-TRUST-05` | Trusts character quickly and competence never |
| `B39-L13-TRUST-06` | Trusts by institution: the office is trusted, the person is not |
| `B39-L13-TRUST-07` | Trusts by vouching; will trust anyone a trusted person vouches for |
| `B39-L13-TRUST-08` | Never trusts, and functions entirely on verification and procedure |
| `B39-L13-TRUST-09` | Trusts in specific domains only and keeps the boundaries explicit |
| `B39-L13-TRUST-10` | Trusts, and separately prepares for the trust to fail, without contradiction |
| `B39-L13-TRUST-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L13-CONFLICT-01` | Direct confrontation, immediately, and considers delay dishonest |
| `B39-L13-CONFLICT-02` | Avoidance; leaves the room and the matter unresolved for as long as possible |
| `B39-L13-CONFLICT-03` | Accommodation; concedes and carries the resentment privately |

| `B39-L13-CONFLICT-08` | Wins the argument and repairs the relationship separately and deliberately |

| `B39-L13-CONFLICT-10` | Concedes the point and continues the behavior |

| Code | Option |
|---|---|

#### `L13-BETRAY` — Response to being betrayed

| Code | Option |
|---|---|

| `B39-L13-BETRAY-03` | Forgives fully, once, and states that it was once |

| `B39-L13-BETRAY-08` | Converts it to procedure: a rule is made so it cannot happen again |
| `B39-L13-BETRAY-09` | Tells everyone, immediately, and the telling is the retaliation |
| `B39-L13-BETRAY-10` | Cannot recognize betrayal; reinterprets it until it is not one |
| `B39-L13-BETRAY-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

### 17.3 — Cross-References

Part 29 Areas 5 and 14 govern the speech and non-speech social layer L13 supplies conduct to; Part 23 governs formal bonds; Part 27 Area 4 governs Trust architecture, Vendetta and Enmity Formation, which `L13-BETRAY` renders but does not grade; Layer L19 governs the collective code L13 conduct is read against.

## Area 18 — Layer L14: Relational Behavior Matrix

**Group:** IV — People · **Layer code:** `L14` · **Dimensions:** 6 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** the per-pair override table. L14 does not carry a general option catalog in the manner of the other Layers, because its content is relationship-specific by construction. It carries six structural dimensions that are registered once per named relationship.

**Boundary:** Part 23 owns the formal bond and its obligations; Parts 17/18/19 own mentor, ally, rival and companion relationship types; Part 29 Area 16 owns address terms within the relationship. L14 owns the behavioral delta only.

### 18.1 — The Delta Principle

**Rule 39.18.1.0 — Two Free-Text Dimensions.** `L14-PHYSICAL` and `L14-FORBIDDEN` below carry no coded option table. They are the only two dimensions in Part 39 that do not, and the exception is recorded in Rule 39.3.2.1. Their values are registered as prose and count as addressed under Rule 39.3.3.1 on the same terms as any coded dimension.

**Rule 39.18.1.1 — L14 Records Differences, Not Duplicates.** A relational entry states only where this character's conduct departs from their registered baseline in the presence of that specific person. A relationship producing no departure is recorded as `no delta` and that is a meaningful registration, not an empty one: it says this person is the same with their sovereign as with a stranger, which is itself a character fact.

**Rule 39.18.1.2 — Relational Entries Arise From the Story.** A character above Tier C registers an L14 entry for a relationship when the story shows that it matters; the usual ones are a parent or guardian living or dead, a child or ward, an immediate superior, an immediate subordinate or student, a registered rival, and a closest bond. Whether a relationship matters is judged at Gate 2 from what the story has shown, and the entry is registered at the interview (Area 45). There is no list that must be complete, no count of shared Areas decides which entries exist, and nothing caps their number.

**Rule 39.18.1.3 — Asymmetry Is Required.** Two characters' L14 entries about each other are written independently and are not required to agree. The Grand Marshal's delta toward a clerk and that clerk's delta toward the Grand Marshal describe different experiences of the same relationship, and their mismatch is frequently the most interesting thing in it.

### 18.2 — Dimension Catalog

Each dimension below is registered once per named relationship, in the form `[Person] — [dimension]: [value]`.

#### `L14-DELTA` — Which baseline behaviors change in this person's presence

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L14-DELTA-01` | No delta; baseline conduct is unchanged |
| `B39-L14-DELTA-02` | Physical register changes (L01 dimensions named explicitly) |
| `B39-L14-DELTA-03` | Speech-adjacent register changes (L10 dimensions named explicitly) |
| `B39-L14-DELTA-04` | Emotional expression opens beyond its usual latency (L03) |
| `B39-L14-DELTA-05` | Emotional expression closes below its usual level (L03) |
| `B39-L14-DELTA-06` | Moral reflex is suspended or overridden for this person (L08) |
| `B39-L14-DELTA-07` | Humor register opens that is closed to everyone else (L11) |
| `B39-L14-DELTA-08` | Decision behavior changes: defers, or ceases to defer (L06) |
| `B39-L14-DELTA-09` | Concealment posture changes: tells them everything, or uniquely nothing (L09) |
| `B39-L14-DELTA-10` | Reverts to an earlier, superseded version of themselves entirely |
| `B39-L14-DELTA-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L14-TRIGGER` — What in this person produces the delta

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L14-TRIGGER-01` | Rank or office held |
| `B39-L14-TRIGGER-02` | Shared history of a named event |
| `B39-L14-TRIGGER-03` | Debt owed, in either direction |
| `B39-L14-TRIGGER-04` | Resemblance to a third person, living or dead |
| `B39-L14-TRIGGER-05` | Their competence, admired or resented |
| `B39-L14-TRIGGER-06` | Responsibility for them |
| `B39-L14-TRIGGER-07` | Fear of them, acknowledged or not |
| `B39-L14-TRIGGER-08` | Attraction, acknowledged or not (L17 interface) |
| `B39-L14-TRIGGER-09` | An unresolved grievance |
| `B39-L14-TRIGGER-10` | Nothing identifiable; the delta is temperamental and neither party understands it |
| `B39-L14-TRIGGER-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L14-PHYSICAL` — The specific physical marker of this relationship

Registered as free text with a code from the L01 catalog where one applies. A relationship with no physical marker is recorded `none`, and a character usually registers a few relationships that do have one (two is the guide).

#### `L14-FORBIDDEN` — What is never said or done in this person's presence

Registered as free text. This dimension carries disproportionate narrative weight: the subject a character will not raise with one specific person is a scene waiting to happen.

#### `L14-STABILITY` — Whether the delta is stable or is itself moving

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L14-STABILITY-01` | Stable; has held for years and is expected to hold |
| `B39-L14-STABILITY-02` | Eroding; the delta is smaller than it was and the change is not acknowledged |
| `B39-L14-STABILITY-03` | Deepening; the delta is growing |
| `B39-L14-STABILITY-04` | Newly formed within the current Section |
| `B39-L14-STABILITY-05` | Contingent on a fact one party does not know |
| `B39-L14-STABILITY-06` | Reversing; the relationship is inverting and the conduct has not caught up |
| `B39-L14-STABILITY-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L14-WITNESS` — How the delta changes when a third party is present

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L14-WITNESS-01` | Unchanged; the conduct is the same observed or not |
| `B39-L14-WITNESS-02` | Delta disappears entirely in company and returns in private |
| `B39-L14-WITNESS-03` | Delta appears only in company and is absent in private |
| `B39-L14-WITNESS-04` | Delta changes by who the third party is, with the categories named |
| `B39-L14-WITNESS-05` | Both parties perform an agreed public version neither has discussed |
| `B39-L14-WITNESS-06` | The third party's presence is itself the reason the relationship functions |
| `B39-L14-WITNESS-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

### 18.3 — Cross-References

Part 17 Area 8 governs hero relationship types; Part 18 Area 11 governs the five villain-hero relationship types; Part 19 Area 2 governs side-character narrative functions; Part 23 governs bonds; Part 29 Area 16 governs address within the relationship; Part 27 Area 4 governs the trust and enmity that L14 renders.

## Area 19 — Layer L15: Leadership and Command Behavior

**Group:** IV — People · **Layer code:** `L15` · **Dimensions:** 9 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** how authority is physically and socially performed — order-giving form, delegation, handling of dissent, blame and credit, holding a room, conduct while being reported to, decision announcement, treatment of a subordinate's failure, and how command degrades under loss.

**Boundary:** Part 16 Areas 4–6 own military and sect rank and the authority it confers; Part 22 owns faction command structures; Part 5 Area 11 owns leader death and command continuity. L15 owns the personal conduct of command and never confers authority a character does not hold.

**Applicability:** every character above Tier C registers L15, including those with no command. A character who holds no authority registers how they behave in the one situation where they briefly do — a junior clerk directing a porter, a child instructing a younger child — because that moment is diagnostic.

### 19.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.19.1.1 — Command Is Rendered in the Room's Response.** Authority is shown by what the room does, not by narration asserting that the character commands respect. A leader whose orders are followed only because the prose says they are has no L15 registration in force.

### 19.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L15-ORDER` — Form of an order

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L15-ORDER-01` | Imperative and bare; the instruction with nothing attached |
| `B39-L15-ORDER-02` | Framed as a question that everyone correctly hears as an order |
| `B39-L15-ORDER-03` | States the objective and leaves the method entirely to the subordinate |
| `B39-L15-ORDER-04` | States the method exactly and does not explain the objective |
| `B39-L15-ORDER-05` | Names the person first, then the task, always in that order |
| `B39-L15-ORDER-06` | Gives orders in writing wherever possible |
| `B39-L15-ORDER-07` | Gives the order and states what will happen if it fails |
| `B39-L15-ORDER-08` | Asks whether it can be done before ordering it done |
| `B39-L15-ORDER-09` | Orders by demonstration: begins the task and others join |
| `B39-L15-ORDER-10` | Softens every order with a courtesy that subordinates have learned to ignore |
| `B39-L15-ORDER-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L15-DELEG` — Delegation behavior

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L15-DELEG-01` | Delegates completely and does not check until the deadline |
| `B39-L15-DELEG-02` | Delegates and checks constantly, and knows this undermines it |
| `B39-L15-DELEG-03` | Delegates the visible work and keeps the difficult work themselves |
| `B39-L15-DELEG-04` | Delegates the difficult work deliberately, as training |
| `B39-L15-DELEG-05` | Cannot delegate; takes on more than they can hold and fails predictably |
| `B39-L15-DELEG-06` | Delegates to the same trusted few and starves everyone else of experience |
| `B39-L15-DELEG-07` | Delegates by rota, strictly, regardless of aptitude |
| `B39-L15-DELEG-08` | Delegates authority as well as task, explicitly and in front of witnesses |
| `B39-L15-DELEG-09` | Delegates the task and withholds the authority, then criticizes the result |
| `B39-L15-DELEG-10` | Delegates upward when they can, and it is noticed |
| `B39-L15-DELEG-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L15-DISSENT` — Handling dissent

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L15-DISSENT-01` | Invites it explicitly before deciding and closes it absolutely after |
| `B39-L15-DISSENT-02` | Permits it in private only, and punishes it in public |
| `B39-L15-DISSENT-03` | Permits it in public and treats the public airing as the point |
| `B39-L15-DISSENT-04` | Requires it: assigns someone to argue the opposite case |
| `B39-L15-DISSENT-05` | Hears it and never visibly changes position, though sometimes the decision quietly shifts |
| `B39-L15-DISSENT-06` | Treats it as insubordination regardless of setting |
| `B39-L15-DISSENT-07` | Argues it out fully and personally, as an equal, for as long as it takes |
| `B39-L15-DISSENT-08` | Defers the decision whenever there is dissent, sometimes indefinitely |
| `B39-L15-DISSENT-09` | Records the dissent formally so that the dissenter is protected if they were right |
| `B39-L15-DISSENT-10` | Remembers the dissenter and adjusts their prospects without ever saying so |
| `B39-L15-DISSENT-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L15-BLAME` — Blame and credit behavior

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L15-BLAME-01` | Takes all blame publicly and assigns it privately |
| `B39-L15-BLAME-02` | Assigns blame accurately, publicly, and without heat |
| `B39-L15-BLAME-03` | Takes all blame and never assigns it, even where it is deserved |
| `B39-L15-BLAME-04` | Gives all credit away and is uncomfortable receiving any |
| `B39-L15-BLAME-05` | Takes credit as the office's due and does not consider it personal |
| `B39-L15-BLAME-06` | Names the individual for credit and the unit for blame |
| `B39-L15-BLAME-07` | Records both precisely in writing and refers back to the record |
| `B39-L15-BLAME-08` | Blames the process rather than any person, invariably |
| `B39-L15-BLAME-09` | Takes credit unconsciously and is genuinely unaware of doing it |
| `B39-L15-BLAME-10` | Refuses to discuss either, and the refusal frustrates everyone above and below |
| `B39-L15-BLAME-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L15-ROOM` — Holding a room

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L15-ROOM-01` | By stillness; does not move and the room orders itself around that |
| `B39-L15-ROOM-02` | By movement; circulates and the room's attention follows |
| `B39-L15-ROOM-03` | By first speech; frames the matter before anyone else can |
| `B39-L15-ROOM-04` | By last speech; lets everyone exhaust themselves and then decides |
| `B39-L15-ROOM-05` | By position: takes a specific place and its meaning is understood |
| `B39-L15-ROOM-06` | By object: a document, a seal, a tool placed on the table |
| `B39-L15-ROOM-07` | By naming everyone present, in order, before beginning |
| `B39-L15-ROOM-08` | Does not hold the room and does not try; authority is exercised afterward, elsewhere |
| `B39-L15-ROOM-09` | By volume and physical scale, which they are aware of and use |
| `B39-L15-ROOM-10` | By the timing of their arrival rather than anything done once present |
| `B39-L15-ROOM-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L15-RECV` — Conduct while being reported to

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L15-RECV-01` | Silent and motionless until the report is finished, then one question |
| `B39-L15-RECV-02` | Interrupts constantly to clarify and the reporter never finishes |
| `B39-L15-RECV-03` | Takes notes and the notes are consulted later, visibly |
| `B39-L15-RECV-04` | Continues other work throughout, and proves afterward that they heard it |
| `B39-L15-RECV-05` | Asks for the worst item first and refuses the preamble |
| `B39-L15-RECV-06` | Asks what the reporter recommends before offering any view |
| `B39-L15-RECV-07` | Reacts visibly and immediately, so the reporter can read the reception |
| `B39-L15-RECV-08` | Reacts to nothing, so the reporter cannot |
| `B39-L15-RECV-09` | Sends the reporter back for what is missing rather than working with a partial report |
| `B39-L15-RECV-10` | Thanks the reporter by name and it is the one courtesy they never omit |
| `B39-L15-RECV-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L15-ANNOUNCE` — Announcing a decision

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L15-ANNOUNCE-01` | States the decision and the reasoning, in that order, once |
| `B39-L15-ANNOUNCE-02` | States the decision and withholds the reasoning deliberately |
| `B39-L15-ANNOUNCE-03` | Builds the reasoning aloud so the room reaches the decision with them |
| `B39-L15-ANNOUNCE-04` | Announces to the affected party first and the room afterward |
| `B39-L15-ANNOUNCE-05` | Announces in writing and takes no questions |
| `B39-L15-ANNOUNCE-06` | Announces and immediately asks what they have missed |
| `B39-L15-ANNOUNCE-07` | Announces the decision as the institution's rather than their own |
| `B39-L15-ANNOUNCE-08` | Announces and names who will execute each part before the room disperses |
| `B39-L15-ANNOUNCE-09` | Announces reluctantly, with the cost stated |
| `B39-L15-ANNOUNCE-10` | Does not announce; the decision becomes apparent through what changes |
| `B39-L15-ANNOUNCE-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L15-FAIL` — Handling a subordinate's failure

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L15-FAIL-01` | Corrects immediately and privately, then never refers to it again |
| `B39-L15-FAIL-02` | Requires the subordinate to explain it to the affected party themselves |
| `B39-L15-FAIL-03` | Repairs it personally and says nothing |
| `B39-L15-FAIL-04` | Removes them from the task permanently, without discussion |
| `B39-L15-FAIL-05` | Gives them the same task again immediately |
| `B39-L15-FAIL-06` | Investigates the conditions that produced it before addressing the person |
| `B39-L15-FAIL-07` | Escalates it formally, on principle, even where discretion is available |
| `B39-L15-FAIL-08` | Is disappointed rather than angry, and the disappointment is worse |
| `B39-L15-FAIL-09` | Names their own comparable failure from years ago |
| `B39-L15-FAIL-10` | Cannot address it at all and lets it stand, which subordinates learn to exploit |
| `B39-L15-FAIL-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L15-DEGRADE` — Command behavior under loss

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L15-DEGRADE-01` | Tightens: control narrows, orders shorten, delegation stops |
| `B39-L15-DEGRADE-02` | Loosens: delegates more and steps back, deliberately or otherwise |
| `B39-L15-DEGRADE-03` | Becomes personally reckless while ordering others to be careful |
| `B39-L15-DEGRADE-04` | Becomes cautious to the point of paralysis |
| `B39-L15-DEGRADE-05` | Becomes ceremonial: falls back on protocol as the last available structure |
| `B39-L15-DEGRADE-06` | Goes forward personally; command by presence, at real risk |
| `B39-L15-DEGRADE-07` | Insists on counting and naming the losses before continuing |
| `B39-L15-DEGRADE-08` | Refuses to speak of the losses at all and drives the survivors harder |
| `B39-L15-DEGRADE-09` | Seeks relief; asks to be replaced, sincerely |
| `B39-L15-DEGRADE-10` | Unchanged in conduct, and the invariance frightens their subordinates |
| `B39-L15-DEGRADE-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

### 19.3 — Cross-References

Part 16 Areas 4–6 own rank; Part 22 owns faction command; Part 5 Area 11 owns leader death and command continuity, which `L15-DEGRADE` renders; Part 26 Area 5.3 governs the psychological state under which command degrades; Part 29 Area 5 governs the speech of command.

## Area 20 — Layer L16: Parenting, Caregiving and Mentorship Behavior

**Group:** IV — People · **Layer code:** `L16` · **Dimensions:** 8 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** conduct toward those in their care — children, wards, students, patients, the injured and the dependent. Correction style, what is permitted and forbidden, teaching mode, conduct when the dependent fails, physical care register, what they transmit deliberately, what they transmit without meaning to, and how the caregiving persists after the dependency ends.

**Boundary:** Part 29 Area 4.3.3 owns Mentor *voice*; Part 19 Area 2 owns the Mentor narrative function and Mentor Death Law; Part 17 Area 8 owns hero mentor bonds. L16 owns conduct. The distinction matters: two mentors may share a voice register and differ completely in whether they let a student fail.

**Applicability:** registered by every character above Tier C, including those with no dependents. A character with none registers `L16-CORRECT`, `L16-TEACH` and `L16-PHYS` against the nearest analogue — a junior colleague, a new recruit, an animal — because the conduct exists whether or not the role does.

### 20.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.20.1.1 — Transmission Is Shown, Not Stated.** The most powerful L16 rendering is the moment a dependent reproduces a caregiver's registered behavior without either of them noticing. Where a caregiver and a dependent are both registered, a transmission may be planned in the Part 38 Behavioral Plan across their shared arc.

**Rule 39.20.1.2 — The Unintended Transmission.** Every caregiver registers `L16-UNMEANT` — the thing they pass on that they did not choose to. This is the dimension that makes families legible, and it is required, not optional, for any character with a registered child or student.

### 20.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L16-CORRECT` — Correction style

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L16-CORRECT-01` | Names the error precisely, once, and does not repeat it |
| `B39-L16-CORRECT-02` | Corrects by demonstrating the right way and saying nothing about the wrong one |
| `B39-L16-CORRECT-03` | Asks a question that makes the dependent locate the error themselves |
| `B39-L16-CORRECT-04` | Corrects everything, continuously, including things that do not matter |
| `B39-L16-CORRECT-05` | Corrects only what is dangerous and lets the rest stand |
| `B39-L16-CORRECT-06` | Corrects in private always, and defends the dependent in public without exception |
| `B39-L16-CORRECT-07` | Corrects publicly, believing the shame is part of the instruction |
| `B39-L16-CORRECT-08` | Corrects by consequence: lets the error run to its result and then discusses it |
| `B39-L16-CORRECT-09` | Corrects physically — a hand repositioned, a stance adjusted, a tool taken and returned |
| `B39-L16-CORRECT-10` | Cannot correct at all; praises and hopes, and the dependent suffers for it |
| `B39-L16-CORRECT-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L16-PERMIT` — What is permitted and forbidden

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L16-PERMIT-01` | Wide latitude with one absolute prohibition, stated once and never repeated |
| `B39-L16-PERMIT-02` | Narrow latitude with many rules, all of them explained |
| `B39-L16-PERMIT-03` | Narrow latitude with many rules, none of them explained |
| `B39-L16-PERMIT-04` | Permits danger and forbids dishonesty |
| `B39-L16-PERMIT-05` | Permits dishonesty and forbids danger |
| `B39-L16-PERMIT-06` | Permits failure and forbids not trying |
| `B39-L16-PERMIT-07` | Permits everything and intervenes only at the last possible moment |
| `B39-L16-PERMIT-08` | Rules change with the caregiver's mood, and the dependent has learned to read it |
| `B39-L16-PERMIT-09` | Permits what they themselves were forbidden, deliberately and consciously |
| `B39-L16-PERMIT-10` | Forbids exactly what they themselves did, and will not say why |
| `B39-L16-PERMIT-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L16-TEACH` — Teaching mode

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L16-TEACH-01` | Drill: repetition to the point of reflex, with no explanation until it is automatic |
| `B39-L16-TEACH-02` | First principles: nothing is practised until the reason for it is understood |
| `B39-L16-TEACH-03` | Apprenticeship: the student watches and is given increasing parts of the real work |
| `B39-L16-TEACH-04` | Socratic: teaches entirely by question and never states a conclusion |
| `B39-L16-TEACH-05` | Narrative: teaches by cases and precedents, and the student assembles the rule |
| `B39-L16-TEACH-06` | Immersion: throws the student into the situation and stands ready to catch them |
| `B39-L16-TEACH-07` | Sequential and rigid: no step is taken before the previous one is perfect |
| `B39-L16-TEACH-08` | Teaches only what the student asks for, and withholds the rest deliberately |
| `B39-L16-TEACH-09` | Teaches the trade's ethic before its technique, and will not reverse the order |
| `B39-L16-TEACH-10` | Cannot teach; is highly competent and entirely unable to transmit it |
| `B39-L16-TEACH-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L16-DEPFAIL` — When the dependent fails

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L16-DEPFAIL-01` | Treats it as their own failure of instruction, aloud |
| `B39-L16-DEPFAIL-02` | Withdraws visibly, and the withdrawal is the punishment |
| `B39-L16-DEPFAIL-03` | Increases the workload immediately, before the failure is discussed |
| `B39-L16-DEPFAIL-04` | Sits with them in it, without instruction, until the worst of it passes |
| `B39-L16-DEPFAIL-05` | Requires them to repair it themselves, entirely and publicly |
| `B39-L16-DEPFAIL-06` | Repairs it themselves and lets the dependent watch |
| `B39-L16-DEPFAIL-07` | Tells them about a comparable failure of their own |
| `B39-L16-DEPFAIL-08` | Lowers expectations permanently and never announces that they have |
| `B39-L16-DEPFAIL-09` | Cannot conceal disappointment and the dependent reads it instantly |
| `B39-L16-DEPFAIL-10` | Is unmoved; failure is data and carries no relational weight at all |
| `B39-L16-DEPFAIL-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L16-PHYS` — Physical care register

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L16-PHYS-01` | Competent and matter-of-fact; injuries dressed, hair tied, coats fastened, without comment |
| `B39-L16-PHYS-02` | Avoids all physical care and delegates it, uncomfortably |
| `B39-L16-PHYS-03` | Only in emergency; the register is opened by injury and closed the moment it heals |
| `B39-L16-PHYS-04` | Formal and ceremonious even in care, which reads as either dignity or coldness |
| `B39-L16-PHYS-05` | Physically affectionate without restraint and without regard for setting |
| `B39-L16-PHYS-06` | One specific gesture, used consistently, and no other contact at all |
| `B39-L16-PHYS-07` | Rough and effective; care delivered in the manner of handling equipment |
| `B39-L16-PHYS-08` | Present but not touching; sits nearby for as long as it takes |
| `B39-L16-PHYS-09` | Feeds them; the whole register of care is expressed through food |
| `B39-L16-PHYS-10` | Cannot bear to see them hurt and becomes useless, which they know and plan around |
| `B39-L16-PHYS-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L16-TRANSMIT` — What they deliberately pass on

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L16-TRANSMIT-01` | A craft, completely, with the expectation that it will be exceeded |
| `B39-L16-TRANSMIT-02` | A standard: what is acceptable work, and what is not, at any cost |
| `B39-L16-TRANSMIT-03` | A caution: the specific danger that took someone from them |
| `B39-L16-TRANSMIT-04` | A duty: an obligation the dependent did not ask for and cannot refuse |
| `B39-L16-TRANSMIT-05` | An independence: everything necessary to not need them |
| `B39-L16-TRANSMIT-06` | A loyalty, to a person, an office, or a house |
| `B39-L16-TRANSMIT-07` | A specific object, and everything the object means |
| `B39-L16-TRANSMIT-08` | A question they never answered, handed on unresolved |
| `B39-L16-TRANSMIT-09` | Nothing deliberately; they refuse to shape the dependent at all, as a position |
| `B39-L16-TRANSMIT-10` | Their own unfinished work, explicitly, and the weight of it |
| `B39-L16-TRANSMIT-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L16-UNMEANT` — What they transmit without meaning to

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
