## Area 21 — Layer L17: Romance, Intimacy and Attraction Behavior

**Group:** IV — People · **Layer code:** `L17` · **Dimensions:** 8 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** how attraction shows before it is admitted, courtship conduct within the cultural frame, physical affection norms, conduct in an established bond, jealousy behavior, behavior in a partner's absence, how the intimate register differs from every other, and conduct at the end of a bond.

**Boundary:** Part 17 Rule 17.8.6 owns hero romance law and its permissions; Part 25 owns the cultural paradigm and its social conventions and taboos (Areas 9 to 11), which courtship works within, and has no separate courtship frame; Part 16 Area 16's young-character bands govern what is permissible at a given age. L17 grants nothing: a registered attraction behavior does not authorize a romance Part 17 does not permit, and no L17 registration may be made for a character in the YC-1 or YC-2 bands beyond `B39-L17-STAGE-01`.

### 21.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.21.1.1 — Attraction Is Rendered as Deviation.** Attraction reaches the page as a departure from the character's own registered baseline — the person who is never still becoming still, the person who always finishes a sentence not finishing one. It is never rendered as narration reporting that the character is attracted.

**Rule 39.21.1.2 — Age Gate.** For any character in Part 16's YC-1 or YC-2 bands, the whole Layer registers as `B39-L17-STAGE-01` (age-gated; not applicable) and no other L17 dimension is populated. The Layer opens only when the character reaches age 17, the start of Part 16's YC-3 band, and the crossing is itself a Conditional Drift Map entry (Area 42). The seven unpopulated dimensions are `[N-A]` by this rule; they count as addressed, and a closed L17 counts toward 36 of 36 Layers.

**Rule 39.21.1.3 — Stage Pace Is Guidance.** The L17-STAGE ladder sets no minimum gap between stages. Part 17 Rule 17.8.6.7 gives typical pacing (about 3 Sections to a first crisis or resolution) for every named romance, hero, side or villain; the confirmed spec chain and the macro story decide, no check fails on it, and Gate 1 may note a fast stage jump as information only.

## Area 22 — Layer L18: Oath, Promise and Contract Behavior

**Group:** IV — People · **Layer code:** `L18` · **Dimensions:** 7 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** what a binding word means to this person and what they do around it — the weight they assign an oath, the form they use, conduct when two oaths collide, conduct when a promise becomes costly, conduct toward an oath-breaker, what they will and will not swear to, and what they do with an oath they can no longer keep.

**Boundary:** Part 23 owns affiliation agreements and their formal breach; Part 27 owns oath-breach consequence and the vendetta it may open; Part 22 owns faction oaths and who may administer them. L18 owns the personal conduct around the vow. A registered L18 behavior never alters the binding force Part 23 assigns an agreement.

### 22.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.22.1.1 — The Oath Is Rendered at Its Cost.** A vow is characterized not when it is sworn but when keeping it becomes expensive. A character with a registered oath is usually shown, at some point in their arc, paying for it — or visibly choosing not to; the confirmed Chapter Spec decides when.

### 22.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L18-WEIGHT` — What an oath weighs

|---|---|
| `B39-L18-WEIGHT-01` | Absolute; an oath outranks life, office, family and preference without exception |
| `B39-L18-WEIGHT-02` | Absolute in form and negotiable in interpretation, which they do not regard as a contradiction |
| `B39-L18-WEIGHT-03` | Binding while the conditions that produced it hold, and void when they change |
| `B39-L18-WEIGHT-04` | A formal oath binds and a casual promise does not, and the distinction is sharp |
| `B39-L18-WEIGHT-05` | A casual promise binds exactly as much as a formal oath, and they are known for it |
| `B39-L18-WEIGHT-06` | Binding only to those who keep theirs |
| `B39-L18-WEIGHT-07` | An instrument; oaths are given to achieve outcomes and weigh accordingly |
| `B39-L18-WEIGHT-08` | Binding on the office and not the person, and they distinguish carefully |
| `B39-L18-WEIGHT-09` | Weighs by who received it, not by what was sworn |
| `B39-L18-WEIGHT-10` | They do not swear, and this refusal is itself the registered position |

# GROUP V — WORLD (Layers L19–L23)

> **Group V governs the character inside a world that has its own conduct.** L19 is the collective code and the individual's seam against it — the Layer that makes a faction feel like an institution populated by people rather than a monolith with a name. L20 is formal frame; L21 is many eyes; L22 is away from home; L23 is the daily texture that a nine-year Phase is actually made of.

---

## Area 23 — Layer L19: Collective, Faction and World Coupling

**Group:** V — World · **Layer code:** `L19` · **Dimensions:** 9 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** how this character behaves as a member of a group — conformance to the active collective code, the specific seam where they deviate, in-group versus out-group shift, rank-presence modulation, conduct on another faction's ground, cross-faction friction behavior, conduct when their group is criticized, conduct when their group is wrong, and location-conditioned behavior.

**Boundary:** Parts 21, 22, 23 and 25 own civilizations, factions, affiliations and cultural paradigms respectively; Parts 13–15 own locations. L19 owns only the individual's behavioral relationship to those structures. The collective codes themselves are stored on the faction, civilization and location ledgers under the Part 37 Collective Behavior Code block (Area 41.4), not on the character.

### 23.1 — The Collective Code

**Rule 39.23.1.1 — A Collective Code Is a Registered Object.** Any faction, civilization, kingdom-state or location whose members appear together in prose carries a Collective Behavior Code on its own ledger (a kingdom-state's code sits on the Faction Ledger of its ruling state, Part 37 Area 2.6): the shared conduct all members display, the greeting and deference rituals, the in-group markers, the conduct toward outsiders, and, for locations, the behavior the place itself imposes on anyone standing in it. A group whose members share a name and no conduct is not a group in Part 39 terms.

**Rule 39.23.1.2 — The Seam Is Registered.** Every character bound to a collective code registers `L19-SEAM`: the specific, named way their individual conduct departs from the code. A member who conforms perfectly registers `B39-L19-SEAM-01` and that registration carries its own expectation — perfect conformance is usually shown to cost them something, or to be visibly effortful, at some point; the confirmed Chapter Spec decides when. There is no such thing as a costless perfect member.

**Rule 39.23.1.3 — Group Scenes Are Rendered With the Code Visible.** In a scene where three or more members of the same collective are present, the code usually shows: in how they greet, who defers to whom without being told, what they all do that outsiders do not, and how the register changes the moment an outsider enters. A group scene in which the members behave only as individuals is shown as information.

**Rule 39.23.1.4 — Rank Presence.** Where a superior of the same collective enters, every subordinate member present modulates according to their registered `L19-RANKMOD`, and the modulation is visible in the prose before any dialogue acknowledges the arrival.

**Rule 39.23.1.5 — An Unregistered Collective Blocks the Plan.** A chapter spec that plans members of one collective to appear together in an Area, where that collective's Code Status (Area 41.4) is Not yet registered, records that fact as a blocking item. The question is raised at spec build through the Decision Consultation Gate (Rule 39.46.2.5), and the collective's code is registered before the prose is drafted. A writer never invents the code inside the prose.

### 23.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L19-CONFORM` — Degree of conformance to the active code

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L19-CONFORM-01` | Exemplary; holds the code more exactly than the code requires |
| `B39-L19-CONFORM-02` | Standard; conforms without thought, having never examined it |
| `B39-L19-CONFORM-03` | Performative; conforms visibly in public and not at all in private |
| `B39-L19-CONFORM-04` | Selective; conforms on the forms that matter to them and neglects the rest openly |
| `B39-L19-CONFORM-05` | Reluctant; conforms under observation and resents each instance |
| `B39-L19-CONFORM-06` | Reforming; conforms while working openly to change the code |
| `B39-L19-CONFORM-07` | Nostalgic; conforms to an older version of the code the group has moved past |
| `B39-L19-CONFORM-08` | Enforcing; conforms and requires conformance from others, including outside their authority |
| `B39-L19-CONFORM-09` | Dual; holds two collective codes and switches between them cleanly |
| `B39-L19-CONFORM-10` | Nominal; belongs by record only and displays almost none of the conduct |
| `B39-L19-CONFORM-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

#### `L19-SEAM` — The named deviation from the code

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L19-SEAM-01` | No deviation; conformance is total and is shown to be effortful or costly |
| `B39-L19-SEAM-02` | Deviates on one specific ritual, consistently, and has never explained why |
| `B39-L19-SEAM-03` | Deviates in the treatment of outsiders, being warmer than the code permits |
| `B39-L19-SEAM-04` | Deviates in the treatment of outsiders, being colder than the code permits |
| `B39-L19-SEAM-05` | Deviates on deference: gives less than the code requires, or more |
| `B39-L19-SEAM-06` | Retains a conduct from a previous affiliation that does not belong here |
| `B39-L19-SEAM-07` | Retains a conduct from their class or region that the group has trained out of others |
| `B39-L19-SEAM-08` | Deviates only when the code and their moral reflex collide (L08 interface) |
| `B39-L19-SEAM-09` | Deviates in private and conforms absolutely in public, with the seam invisible to the group |
| `B39-L19-SEAM-10` | Deviates openly and has made the deviation a recognized part of their standing |

## Area 24 — Layer L20: Ritual, Ceremony and Protocol Behavior

**Group:** V — World · **Layer code:** `L20` · **Dimensions:** 8 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** conduct inside formal frames — what the hands do during ceremony, precedence instinct, how a bow, seal, signature or oath is physically performed, where they deviate from protocol, conduct when protocol collides with urgency, conduct at funerals and before the dead, observance of their culture's religious or ancestral forms, and their private rituals.

**Boundary:** Part 25 owns cultural and religious forms; Part 21 owns civilizational institutions; Part 29 Area 6 owns formality registers in speech. L20 owns physical and behavioral conduct within the form. It never invents a rite: a registered L20 behavior conforms to, or visibly departs from, a form Part 25 or the relevant ledger has established.

### 24.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.24.1.1 — Ceremony Is Characterized by the Small Deviation.** A perfectly performed rite characterizes nobody. The registration lives in what this person does that the rite does not require: the pause before the seal, the second glance at the signature, the hand that goes to the wrong place first.

### 24.2 — Dimension Catalog

## Area 25 — Layer L21: Crowd and Public Appearance Behavior

**Group:** V — World · **Layer code:** `L21` · **Dimensions:** 7 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** conduct under many eyes — before an assembly, in a market or street, when recognized, when unrecognized, inside a crowd's movement, in a panic or a mob, and what holding the public self costs them afterward.

**Boundary:** Part 21/22 own the political meaning of a public appearance; Part 5 owns combat once violence begins and has no separate crowd rule; Layer L15 owns command; Layer L13 owns the one-to-one mask. L21 owns the many-eyes register specifically, which is a different thing from either.

### 25.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.25.1.1 — The Cost Is Rendered After.** Where a character registers an effortful public self, the prose shows the cost being paid in the private beat that follows: the door closed, the collar loosened, the held posture released. A public performance with no aftermath was not a performance.

## Area 26 — Layer L22: Travel and Away-From-Home Behavior

**Group:** V — World · **Layer code:** `L22` · **Dimensions:** 8 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** conduct outside their own ground — packing and carrying, pace and route discipline, navigation and orientation habit, conduct at inn or camp, sleep away from home, conduct with travelling companions, what they do on arrival, and what they bring back.

**Boundary:** Parts 15 and 32 own travel routes and vehicles; Part 14 owns distances; Parts 13–14 own the places travelled to. L22 owns the traveller's conduct and never establishes a route, a distance, or a means of transport.

### 26.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.26.1.1 — The Road Is a Scene Type, Not a Transition.** Travel is one of the few settings in which characters are unoccupied, unobserved by their institutions, and adjacent to each other for long stretches. Registered L22 conduct is what turns a journey from a paragraph of elapsed distance into a scene.

### 26.2 — Dimension Catalog

|---|---|

| `B39-L22-PACK-02` | Too much, and refuses to be persuaded otherwise |
| `B39-L22-PACK-03` | Carries one item of no practical use that never leaves them |
| `B39-L22-PACK-04` | Packs to a written list, the same list every time |
| `B39-L22-PACK-05` | Packs at the last moment and forgets something specific and predictable |

## Area 27 — Layer L23: Daily-Life and Mundane Behavior

**Group:** V — World · **Layer code:** `L23` · **Dimensions:** 11 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** the ordinary texture of a life — sleep, dress and grooming, money, possessions, the arrangement of their own space, private routine, off-duty occupation, conduct with animals, conduct with children not in their care, superstitions, and what they do on a day with no obligations.

**Boundary:** Parts 21/25 own the material and cultural conditions within which a daily life is possible; Part 11 owns wealth as a canonical resource. L23 owns the habits. It never establishes a resource level: a registered spending behavior is conduct, not an entitlement.

### 27.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.27.1.1 — Nine Years Are Made of Ordinary Days.** A Phase spanning nine in-story years that renders only its crises has rendered a summary, not a life. L23 is the Layer that makes elapsed time legible, and its registrations are the cheapest available source of the Part 36 living-scene standard.

# GROUP VI — PRACTICE AND POWER (Layers L24–L26)

> **Group VI governs the character in relation to their own power.** L24 is how they practise, L25 is how they fight, L26 is what happens to their bearing as the power grows. In a cultivation novel these are the Layers most at risk of being written as mechanics rather than as conduct: the realm is Part 3's, the move is Part 6's, the legality is Part 5's, and none of those tells you whether this person trains before dawn or after midnight, opens a fight or waits to be opened on, or becomes gentler or crueller at the next tier.

---

## Area 28 — Layer L24: Cultivation and Technology Practice Behavior

**Group:** VI — Practice and Power · **Layer code:** `L24` · **Dimensions:** 11 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** how this person practises — session ritual, place and hour, approach to a breakthrough and conduct after it, plateau behavior, relationship to their own affinity, Heart discipline expression, soul-sense usage habit, instrument and technology handling, learning mode, and resource discipline.

**Boundary:** Part 3 owns realms, breakthrough conditions, energy reserves and Qi Deviation mechanics; Part 4 owns affinity, soul and Heart; Part 6 owns techniques; Part 8 owns technology tiers; Part 31 owns synergy. L24 owns only the habit and the conduct. A registered L24 behavior never accelerates a breakthrough, extends a reserve, or grants an affinity.

**Applicability:** every character above Tier C, including non-cultivators and the technologically untrained. A character with neither track registers `L24-SESSION` and `L24-LEARN` against whatever skill they do practise, and registers `L27-POWERLESS` for their relationship to a world of cultivators.

### 28.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.28.1.1 — Practice Is Where Character Is Cheapest to Show.** Training scenes are frequent, low-stakes and structurally repetitive, which makes them the most efficient place in the novel to render a person. What time they choose, what they do first, what they refuse to skip, and what they do when it goes badly, carry more characterization per word than most confrontations.

**Rule 39.28.1.2 — Breakthrough Conduct Is Registered Before It Happens.** A character's approach to and conduct after a breakthrough is registered in advance, and the registration is consulted when Part 3 permits the breakthrough. Breakthroughs are among the highest-drift events in the system (Area 42) and improvising the conduct at the moment wastes the single largest characterization opportunity in a cultivation arc.

### 28.3 — Cross-References

Part 3 owns realms, breakthrough, reserves, environment and Qi Deviation; Part 4 owns affinity, soul and Heart; Part 6 owns technique acquisition; Part 7 owns fusion; Part 8 owns technology tiers; Part 11 owns CPM resources; Part 31 owns synergy and the CS%/TS% values L24 conduct expresses but never sets.

---

## Area 29 — Layer L25: Combat and Fighting Behavior

**Group:** VI — Practice and Power · **Layer code:** `L25` · **Dimensions:** 16 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** fighting as character rather than as mechanics — opening, guard, tempo, range, footwork, target-selection ethic, escalation discipline, Named Move deployment doctrine, energy budgeting, conduct when injured, pain display, killing conduct, mercy and surrender handling, retreat, post-combat ritual, and the change that occurs when they are protecting someone.

**Boundary — this Layer is heavily constrained and the constraints are absolute.** Part 5 owns combat legitimacy, the seven escalation tiers, engagement recognition, protected status, termination, victory and defeat classifications, and post-combat obligations. Part 6 owns move types, access, cost, mastery and collision. Part 3 owns what a realm can physically do. Part 33 owns weapons. **A registered L25 behavior that would breach any of those is invalid on its face** (P39-14). L25 chooses among the conducts each of those Parts permits; it never expands them.

### 29.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.29.1.1 — Fighting Style Is Not Fighting Capability.** Two cultivators at the same realm with the same affinity fight differently because one opens and one waits, one commits and one probes, one finishes a downed opponent and one does not. Capability is Parts 3, 4 and 6. Style is L25, and style is what the reader remembers.

**Rule 39.29.1.2 — The Four Mandatory Combat Elements Interface.** Part 2's combat rules require four elements in every combat scene. L25 supplies the per-character content of those elements. A combat scene that meets Part 2's structural requirement with generic conduct has satisfied the form and failed the character.

**Rule 39.29.1.3 — Combat Drift Is High-Yield.** First kill, first defeat, first mercy given, first mercy received, and first failure to protect are the five highest-drift combat events in the system. First kill and first defeat carry the usual entries of Rule 39.42.4.1; the other three are mapped when the character's arc foresees them and otherwise fire the unmapped-trigger path (Rule 39.42.5.2).

## Area 30 — Layer L26: Power-Scaling Behavioral Governance

**Group:** VI — Practice and Power · **Layer code:** `L26` · **Dimensions:** 7 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** how bearing changes as power changes — the direction of change at each realm or tier crossing, patience, tolerance for insult, treatment of the weaker, what stops frightening them and what starts, and the relationship between their registered power and their registered self-image.

**Boundary:** Part 3 owns the ladder and Part 30 owns the locked end-state levels each protagonist reaches. Part 16 owns classification. L26 owns only the conduct that accompanies movement along that ladder, and never alters the ladder, the pace, or the end-state.

### 30.1 — The Scaling Mandate

**Rule 39.30.1.1 — Every Tier Crossing Requires a Registered Behavioral Consequence.** A character who advances a realm or tier and behaves identically afterward has been given a number, not a promotion. A crossing is a Part 3 Area 4.5 realm transition, or the step from Mortal Tier 1 to Mortal Tier 2; a Level step (Part 3 Area 4.4) or a sub-level step (Part 3 Area 4.3) counts as a crossing only where the character's Conditional Drift Map names it. A crossing may move registered dimensions; where it does, the movement is logged to Section 12 as a drift event with the crossing as its trigger (Area 42). A crossing that moves nothing is lawful as a recorded no-change crossing, for example where the character registers `B39-L26-DIRECTION-07` or the Conditional Drift Map records the crossing as no change with its reason. A no-change crossing is logged as a Section 12 no-change entry, so the choice to stay the same is on record. This is a Check #21 detection class (Area 44).

**Rule 39.30.1.2 — The Direction Is Registered In Advance.** `L26-DIRECTION` states, before the crossing occurs, which way this person moves as they gain power. Two characters crossing the same threshold may become gentler and crueller respectively, and both are legitimate; what is not legitimate is deciding which at the moment of drafting.

**Rule 39.30.1.3 — Scaling Applies Downward.** Loss of power — suppression, injury, sealing, a Firmament-Lock effect, aging — invokes the same Layer in reverse, and `L26-LOSS` registers that conduct separately. It is not assumed to be the mirror image of the gain.

# GROUP VII — PRESSURE AND CHANGE (Layers L27–L31)

> **Group VII is where the profile stops being a portrait and becomes an arc.** L27 governs how the other live Layers bend under load. L28 governs the aftermath of an outcome. L29 governs the dead time that most writing throws away. L30 governs the nine years of growing up that Phase 1 actually contains. L31 is the drift law itself — the mechanism by which a person changes without becoming a different person.

---

## Area 31 — Layer L27: Stress-State Modulation

**Group:** VII — Pressure and Change · **Layer code:** `L27` · **Dimensions:** 10 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** how the registered profile changes under load — fatigue, grief, fear, rage, shame, victory and praise, powerlessness, and the per-character PIS-tier drift map naming which signatures move at PIS-3, PIS-5 and PIS-7.

**Boundary:** Part 26 owns the psychological event, its grade, its accumulation and its terminal states. L27 owns the observable modulation only. **Rule 39.2.3.2 is absolute here:** no L27 registration sets, implies, or revises a PIS level. The PIS grade is read from Part 26 and the ledger; L27 states what this person looks like at it.

### 31.1 — The Modulation Principle

**Rule 39.31.1.1 — Stress Bends the Profile, It Does Not Replace It.** A character under load is recognizably themselves doing something harder. The registration names which of their own dimensions move and in which direction, never a wholesale substitution of a different person. A character who becomes unrecognizable under stress has not been modulated; they have been swapped.

**Rule 39.31.1.2 — The PIS Ladder Registration Is Mandatory Above Tier C.** `L27-PIS3`, `L27-PIS5` and `L27-PIS7` are registered for every hero, villain and other character above Tier C, naming the specific dimensions that move at each tier and what they move to. This is what allows Part 26's grading to produce visible, consistent, per-character consequence on the page rather than a number in a ledger.

**Rule 39.31.1.3 — Recovery Is Registered Too.** Every L27 registration names what returns the character to baseline and how long it takes. A modulation with no stated recovery is a permanent change, and permanent changes belong in Section 12 under Area 42, not here.

#### `L27-PIS5` — Modulation at PIS-5

At PIS-5 the change is visible to colleagues and strangers, and recovery requires an event rather than time alone. Mandatory for every character above Tier C.

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L27-PIS5-01` | A Signature ceases to fire entirely for the duration |

#### `L27-PIS7` — Modulation at PIS-7

At PIS-7 the change is severe and the profile is at risk of permanent movement. Any PIS-7 event automatically opens a Check #21 enquiry (Area 44). Mandatory for every character above Tier C.

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L27-PIS7-01` | Two or more Signatures cease; the character is visibly not themselves |
| `B39-L27-PIS7-02` | A previously superseded behavior returns in full (P39-10, Area 42) |
| `B39-L27-PIS7-03` | The registered line (L08-LINE) is approached or crossed |
| `B39-L27-PIS7-04` | Relational deltas (L14) collapse; they treat everyone identically |
| `B39-L27-PIS7-05` | Practice stops entirely (L24) |

| `B39-L27-PIS7-08` | Sleep, consumption and body-state discipline all fail together |
| `B39-L27-PIS7-09` | A single fixed act is repeated compulsively in place of function |
| `B39-L27-PIS7-10` | Total behavioral flattening; the person is procedurally intact and personally absent |
| `B39-L27-PIS7-99` | AUTHOR-SPECIFIED |

## Area 32 — Layer L28: Outcome-Response Behavior

**Group:** VII — Pressure and Change · **Layer code:** `L28` · **Dimensions:** 9 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** conduct after an outcome — victory, defeat, humiliation, being spared, sparing another, failing someone who depended on them, unearned success, an outcome they cannot interpret, and the interval before the next attempt.

**Boundary:** Part 27 owns the consequence event, its severity grade, its propagation and its resolution pathways. Part 17 owns hero failure states; Part 18 owns the five villain defeat types. L28 owns the personal conduct in the aftermath and never grades a consequence.

### 32.1 — Why This Layer Exists Separately

Defeat is not stress and is not consequence. Part 26 grades what a defeat does inside; Part 27 grades what it costs outside; neither states what this person *does on the following morning*. That morning is where character is made or lost, and it is the most frequently skipped scene in long narrative. L28 registers it in advance so that it is written rather than summarized.

**Rule 39.32.1.1 — The Morning After Is a Scene.** When the story shows a defeat or a first-instance outcome listed in Rule 39.32.1.2, the prose renders the character's registered L28 conduct in an actual scene rather than reporting it in narration; no grade (CSS-5 or any other) triggers it.

**Rule 39.32.1.2 — First-Instance Outcomes Are Named Drift Triggers.** First defeat carries the usual entry of Rule 39.42.4.1. First victory over a serious opponent, first public humiliation, first time spared, first time sparing, and first failure to protect are mapped when the character's arc foresees them and otherwise fire the unmapped-trigger path (Rule 39.42.5.2).

## Area 33 — Layer L29: Idle, Waiting and Boredom Behavior

**Group:** VII — Pressure and Change · **Layer code:** `L29` · **Dimensions:** 7 · **Mandatory-core:** YES

**Governs:** what this person does when nothing is happening — waiting posture, what the hands find, whether they fill silence, conduct in a queue or an antechamber, conduct in the dead interval before danger, tolerance for their own inactivity, and what they do while another person completes a slow task.

**Boundary:** none of substance. L29 is Part 39's own territory in the fullest sense; no other Part governs the empty minute.

### 33.1 — Why This Layer Is Mandatory-Core

**Rule 39.33.1.1 — Empty Time Is the Cheapest Characterization in the Novel.** Every scene contains intervals in which nothing is required of the character. Mechanical writing deletes those intervals; a living novel fills them with a specific person. A gate warden who appears in one scene and waits in it in a particular way has been characterized more efficiently than one given a paragraph of backstory. This is why L29 sits in the mandatory-core six alongside the body, the voice and the conscience.

**Rule 39.33.1.2 — The Waiting Beat.** An Area containing a named character who is present but not currently acting usually renders a waiting beat from that character's L29 registration. A character present for under half the Area, or one of four or more silent members, may share one collective waiting beat. The report shows it at Check #20 (Area 43, element 20-6) as information; no beat is added to meet it.

## Area 34 — Layer L30: Age-Band Behavioral Governance

**Group:** VII — Pressure and Change · **Layer code:** `L30` · **Dimensions:** 6 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** the behavioral band appropriate to a character's age, the child-anchor behaviors that survive prodigy status, the conduct of each transition between bands, what is lost at each crossing, what is retained from the previous band, and the specific risk of writing a child as a small adult.

**Boundary:** Part 16 owns the young-character bands YC-1, YC-2 and YC-3 and their governance; Part 29 Area 17's MHDAI owns the Child-Anchor Requirement in *speech*. L30 extends the Child-Anchor principle to *conduct* and never redefines a band.

**Why this Layer is load-bearing in Phase 1:** the Phase spans nine in-story years and two of the eight protagonists begin it at age eight and end it at seventeen. Without L30, a character written at eight and a character written at seventeen are distinguished only by their realm.

### 34.1 — The Child-Anchor Extension

**Rule 39.34.1.1 — Conduct Child-Anchors Are Mandatory.** Every character in Part 16's YC-1 or YC-2 band registers at least three `L30-ANCHOR` behaviors: conduct that is unmistakably of their age and that persists regardless of talent, realm, rank, or precocity. Part 29 Area 17 established this principle for prodigy speech; Rule 39.34.1.1 extends it to the body, the attention, the appetite and the fear. A prodigy who cultivates like an adult, speaks like an adult and also *waits, eats, sulks and tires* like an adult is not a prodigy; they are an adult who has been assigned a low number. Each Area in which such a character appears renders at least one registered anchor (Check #20 element 20-11). For a Tier D child `L30-ANCHOR` is open: the anchors are three or more `B39-L30-ANCHOR` codes in the Code cell (Rule 39.3.5.3), and no Section 12 exists to retire them, so only the author changes the band.

**Rule 39.34.1.2 — Anchors Are Retired Explicitly, Not Silently.** A child-anchor behavior does not simply stop appearing as the character ages. Its retirement is a Conditional Drift Map entry with the age or event that ends it, logged to Section 12 (Area 42). The last appearance of a retired anchor is a scene worth planning. The minimum of three applies at registration; each retirement names the anchors that remain, and a YC-1 or YC-2 character never holds fewer than one live anchor.

**Rule 39.34.1.3 — Band Crossing and Registered Change.** A crossing between bands usually moves registered dimensions, in directions stated in advance (two is the guide). This is a Check #21 detection class (Area 44).

### 34.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L30-BAND` — Current band

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L30-BAND-01` | Young child (Part 16 YC-1) |
| `B39-L30-BAND-02` | Adolescent (Part 16 YC-2) |
| `B39-L30-BAND-03` | Young adult (Part 16 YC-3) |

## Area 35 — Layer L31: Behavioral Evolution and Drift Law

**Group:** VII — Pressure and Change · **Layer code:** `L31` · **Dimensions:** 7 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** the per-character parameters of change — which behaviors are immutable and which are plastic, the character's rate of change, their permanence bias, their reversion profile, their awareness of their own change, how others register it, and the drift count, which shows as information how many dimensions have moved.

**Boundary:** this Layer holds the *per-character settings*. The *mechanism* — the Conditional Drift Map, its trigger taxonomy, its permanence grades and its logging format — is Area 42, and the *detection* is Area 44's Check #21. L31 is consulted by both.

### 35.1 — The Immutable Core

**Rule 39.35.1.1 — Every Character Has an Immutable Core.** Two registered Signatures are usually designated immutable (a guide): they do not move for any trigger, at any PIS tier, across the whole of the character's arc. The immutable core is what makes a transformed character still recognizable, and its absence is what makes a long arc read as a sequence of different people wearing one name. Where a story genuinely requires an immutable behavior to break, that break is a Canon Amendment matter (Area 49), not a drift event.

**Rule 39.35.1.2 — The Plastic Set Is Named.** The dimensions eligible to move are named in advance. A dimension outside the plastic set that appears to have moved in prose is a Check #20 conformance failure, not a drift event, unless the author rules otherwise at Check #21.

**Rule 39.35.1.3 — The Drift Count.** How many dimensions a registered profile moves in a Section follows the story. The Section 12 header shows the number moved in the Section as information, with no cap and no suspension rule. A character who changes continuously reads as unstable, and the author decides whether that is intended.

# GROUP VIII — IDENTITY GOVERNANCE (Layers L32–L36)

> **Group VIII is the governance half of the Part.** These five Layers carry fewer dimensions than the rest and more binding rules. L32 handles non-baseline-human conduct. L33 governs how other POVs are permitted to perceive the character. L34 requires every designated Signature to name where it came from. L35 is retired (P39-12; Area 39). L36 supplies the counted anti-mechanical notes that Check #20 shows as information. L34 is mandatory-core at every tier.

---

## Area 36 — Layer L32: Species, Hybrid and Non-Human Behavioral Governance

**Group:** VIII — Identity Governance · **Layer code:** `L32` · **Dimensions:** 5 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** behavioral governance for characters who are not baseline human — hybrid sentients, bipedal sentient species, other sentient beings, and constructed or awakened intelligences. Non-baseline kinesics, species-conditioned social conduct, the relationship between species conduct and adopted cultural conduct, conduct toward baseline humans, and the specific hazard of writing a non-human as a human with a feature.

**Boundary:** Part 16 Areas 17–19 own hybrid sentient governance, bipedal species governance and the SADS scale, and other-sentient-being governance; Part 16 Area 1 owns the sentience criterion that determines whether an entity is a character at all. L32 owns conduct only, and never establishes a species, a SADS position, or a sentience determination.

Which characters are non-baseline is read from each ledger `L32-TYPE` and Part 16, never from this Area.

**Monsters.** A sentient monster (Part 40 Area 11) is a non-baseline character whose `L32-TYPE` is `B39-L32-TYPE-06`, Other sentient being, and it is registered in full. A non-sentient monster is not a character and takes no `L32-TYPE`; the Part 40 trait lines reuse dimensions of this Layer and others by pointer, and Rule 39.36.1.1 carries over to them as Part 40 Rule 40.5.3.1.

**Consequence for registration.** For every non-baseline character, `L32-TYPE` is a live registration and Rule 39.36.1.1 binds: several dimensions **across the other live Layers** (three is the guide) carry values unavailable to a baseline human or available on different terms. A hybrid whose species shows only in Layer L32 and nowhere else has been written as a human with a feature, which is precisely what this Layer exists to prevent.

### 36.1 — Rendering Rule

**Rule 39.36.1.1 — Difference Is Structural, Not Decorative.** A non-baseline character whose only non-human conduct is an appearance detail and a single exotic mannerism has been written as a human with a feature. The registration expects several dimensions (three is the guide) across the other live Layers whose values are *unavailable to baseline humans* or *available on different terms* — a different resting posture because the body is differently jointed, a different proxemic default because the sensory range differs, a different idle behavior because the metabolism differs.

**Rule 39.36.1.2 — Species Conduct and Cultural Conduct Are Distinguished.** A hybrid raised entirely within a human kingdom carries that kingdom's L19 collective code and their species' L32 conduct simultaneously, and the friction between them is registered explicitly under `L32-FRICTION`. Collapsing the two into one is the most common failure in writing hybrid characters.

## Area 37 — Layer L33: Read-By-Others Registry

**Group:** VIII — Identity Governance · **Layer code:** `L33` · **Dimensions:** 5 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** how other POV characters are permitted to perceive this person — the physical detail every observer notices, the trait most commonly misread and what it is misread as, the differing readings by rank and faction, the reading held by those who know them best, and the gap between reputation and conduct.

**Boundary:** Part 24 owns reputation as an information asset with a value and a propagation path. L33 owns the perceptual content only. A registered L33 reading never establishes a reputation fact in the information ledger.

### 37.1 — Why This Layer Exists

**Rule 39.37.1.1 — Consistent Description, Divergent Interpretation.** A multi-POV novel must describe a character consistently and interpret them differently. Without a registry, either the descriptions drift — the same person is stiff in one chapter and relaxed in the next with no cause — or the interpretations converge, and every POV reads them identically, which turns other characters into instruments of the narrator. L33 fixes the first and forbids the second.

**Rule 39.37.1.2 — The Misread Is Mandatory.** Every character above Tier C registers `L33-MISREAD`: one registered behavior that is routinely misinterpreted, and the specific wrong conclusion drawn from it. This single field generates more usable inter-character friction than any other in the Part.

**Rule 39.37.1.3 — Revision Is Dated.** When drift (Area 42) changes a registered behavior, the L33 entry is revised and the revision is dated, so that a POV segment set earlier can still render the earlier reading correctly.

### 37.2 — Dimension Catalog

#### `L33-FIRST` — What every observer notices first

| Code | Option |
|---|---|
| `B39-L33-FIRST-01` | Their stillness |
| `B39-L33-FIRST-02` | Their hands, and what is on or in them |
| `B39-L33-FIRST-03` | The condition of their clothing or kit |

## Area 38 — Layer L34: Behavioral Provenance Law

**Group:** VIII — Identity Governance · **Layer code:** `L34` · **Dimensions:** 4 · **Mandatory-core:** YES

**Governs:** the requirement, binding at every registered tier, that every designated Signature name where it came from. Its four dimensions record the origin class of each Signature, the origin's status in canon, whether the character knows the origin, and what would end the behavior.

**Boundary:** Part 28 Area 15's Contextual Earning Law and its Three-Beat Test own the requirement that invented content be earned in prose. L34 is the character-registry expression of the same principle, applied to conduct.

### 38.1 — The Provenance Requirement

**Rule 39.38.1.1 — No Signature Without an Origin.** Every designated Signature carries a stated origin. A Signature with no origin is not registered; it is decoration, and the registration is rejected at Check #20. Part 29 Rule 29.4.4.3 already applies this standard to a single speech act — the prose should know where the speaker got that line, who taught it to them, what trade or schooling ground it into that shape — and Rule 39.38.1.1 generalizes it to conduct.

**Rule 39.38.1.2 — Origins May Be Unknown To the Character and Never To the Ledger.** A character need not know why they do a thing. The ledger always does. `L34-KNOWN` records the difference, and a behavior whose origin is known to the ledger and hidden from the character is one of the most productive structures available: it can be discovered on the page.

**Rule 39.38.1.3 — Origin Constrains Drift.** A behavior's origin determines what could plausibly end it. A habit drilled by an institution ends when the institution's claim ends or is repudiated; a habit formed by a person ends when the relationship to that person changes; a habit formed by an injury ends when the body changes. Area 42 drift entries are checked against `L34-END` for coherence.

**Rule 39.38.1.4 — Provenance Is Not Backstory.** An origin is a single clause, not a history. *Drilled at the muster stone by a warden who counted aloud* is an origin. Three paragraphs about that warden is backstory, belongs in Section 1 or Section 6 if it belongs anywhere, and is not required here.

### 38.2 — Dimension Catalog

## Area 39 — Layer L35: Behavioral Distinctness Law

**Group:** VIII — Identity Governance · **Layer code:** `L35` · **Dimensions:** 3 · **Mandatory-core:** no

Layer L35 is retired. Each character is separate and is registered standalone: no registration is derived from, compared with, cleared against or forbidden to overlap another character's. Overlapping values, including identical Signature pairs, are permitted and need no clearing, no named axis and no exchange. Distinctness is the author's judgment, exercised through the interview (Area 45), and is not a mechanical property of the registry. Agents must not generate comparison material. A conflict is surfaced to the author only when a choice would contradict an explicit citation in a frozen ledger (canon consistency, not distinctness). There is no scene-time swap test and no Check #20 element for this Layer.

**Boundary:** Part 29 Rule 29.4.4.2 tests speech on the page and runs independently (Check #19). Nothing in Area 43 or Area 45 counts or requires a comparison.

**Registration still writes the three lines.** A new registration writes `L35-PAIR`, `L35-SEAM` and `L35-CONVERGE` each as `[N-A]`, citing the retirement of L35 as the stated reason (Rule 39.3.3.1), and records no option and no comparison material. Completeness reads 36/36 Layers and 291/291 dimensions addressed, 288 of them live. The totals are ledger bookkeeping, not a story counter.

---

## Area 40 — Layer L36: Anti-Mechanical Enforcement

**Group:** VIII — Identity Governance · **Layer code:** `L36` · **Dimensions:** 5 · **Mandatory-core:** no

**Governs:** the countable rules that prevent Part 39 from producing a new kind of machine — the contradiction pair, the costly behavior, signature recurrence, the marked off-register note, and the demonstrated-not-named requirement.

**Boundary:** Part 28 Area 6.5's Prohibition on Robotic Behavior and Part 36 Area 17's Prose Vitality Law state the qualitative standard. L36 supplies the counted half, and its elements are shown at Check #20 as information.

### 40.1 — The Failure Mode This Layer Exists To Prevent

A thirty-six-Layer registry creates a specific new risk: a character who executes their registration perfectly in every scene, whose every beat is traceable to a coded option, and who is therefore exactly as mechanical as the unregistered character was — only now with documentation. L36 is the correction. A person is not the consistent output of their specification. They contradict themselves, they act against their interest, they are occasionally not themselves, and their patterns are visible to a reader without ever being announced.

### 40.2 — The Five Rules

**Rule 39.40.2.1 — The Contradiction Pair (guide: two).** Every character above Tier C registers pairs of behaviors in stated tension (two pairs is the guide). A contradiction pair is not a flaw plus a virtue; it is two registered behaviors that cannot both be fully honored, named together with the circumstance in which they collide.

**Rule 39.40.2.2 — The Costly Behavior (guide: one).** A registered behavior demonstrably costs the character something — time, standing, advantage, relationship, or safety — and the cost is shown on the page when the story gives the moment. Section 11.3a records whether it has been (Rule 39.41.5.2).

**Rule 39.40.2.3 — Signature Recurrence (information).** Rule 39.3.3.4 applies: the report shows how often each Signature fired over a run of Areas in which the character appears (Rule 39.3.3.4 counts Areas, not firings); no minimum and no maximum applies.

**Rule 39.40.2.4 — The Off-Register Allowance (guide: one per Section).** A character may act out of character when the story calls for it, and when they do the prose marks it — another character notices, or the character themselves does. An unmarked off-register act is a conformance question; a marked one is characterization. A marked off-register act may contradict only non-Signature dimensions: an act against a registered Signature, marked or not, is a D-1 Signature contradiction (Area 44.3) and goes to Check #21 for the author's ruling. How many occur in a Section is shown as information and no number is a failure.

**Rule 39.40.2.5 — Demonstrated, Never Named (absolute).** No narrator sentence states a Part 39 registration as a property. The narrator does not report that a character is careful, restless, meticulous, or guarded. The registration reaches the page as the act alone. This rule binds narrator voice only, and within narrator voice it admits no exception: a character's spoken judgment of another character, and a POV character's own reading of another character, misreading included (`L33-MISREAD`), are allowed. It is co-extensive with Part 36 Area 17 and Part 28 Area 13.

## Area 41 — Ledger Interface: Character Sections 11 and 12, and the Collective Behavior Code

**Schema authority note.** Part 37 is the schema authority for every ledger file (Part 37 Area 2). Area 41 supplies the *content model* Part 37 incorporates. Where this Area and Part 37 Area 2 disagree on structure, field order, or B/L annotation, **Part 37 governs** and the discrepancy is raised as a Part 39 amendment (Area 49).

### 41.1 — Section 11: Behavioral Signature (B/L)

Section 11 is appended to the Side Character (Part 37 Area 2.1), Villain (Area 2.2) and Hero (Area 2.3) templates, in the thirteen-section character template (Sections 11 and 12 are supplied here; Section 13, Current Condition, follows Section 12 and is supplied by Part 37 Area 2.1). It carries seven blocks in fixed order (with the companion blocks 11.3a and 11.4a), matching the Behavioral Profile Object of Area 3.4.

```markdown

**Part 39 Registration Status:** [Complete — 36/36 Layers addressed, N Signatures | Partial — N/36 | Not yet registered]
**Registration Date:** [YYYY-MM-DD]
**Tier Guide (Part 39 Area 4):** [Hero / Villain / Tier A / Tier B / sentient monster — 36 Layers, 12 Signatures]
**Cross-character comparison:** none registered. Each character is registered separately; overlaps with other characters' registered values are permitted and carry no clearing, axis, or swap-test; any distinction the story needs is the author's to decide, case by case, via AskUserQuestion. No collision-axis material is recorded in this file or in the index.

A `[N-A]` line appends its reason.

**Noticed first:** [value]
**Routinely misread as:** [value]
**By rank:** superiors — [ ] · peers — [ ] · subordinates — [ ] · outsiders — [ ]
**By intimates:** [value]
**Reputation gap:** [value]
**Last revised:** [YYYY-MM-DD — revision reason]

*[L — Sections 11.1 non-signature lines, 11.3a, 11.4, 11.4a, 11.5 and 11.7 are Living. Sections 11.2, 11.3 and 11.6 are Baseline, except that in 11.3 the Off-Register Allowance "used this Section" value and the "paid on the page at" value are Living.]*
```

### 41.2 — Section 12: Behavioral Evolution Log (L)

Section 12 is append-only. Entries are never edited or deleted; a change to a prior entry is made by a new entry that supersedes it and says so. The one exception is rollback, when the Area that caused a drift is archived (Rule 39.41.6.1). The clean rewrite of canon v10.0 folded earlier Section 12 log rows into the body text; no row is restored, and Section 12 is append-only from here on.

```markdown
## Section 12 — Behavioral Evolution Log (L)

**Drift Count (Rule 39.35.1.3):** [N dimensions moved this Section (information only)]
**Immutable Core (L31-IMMUTABLE):** [codes] — unmoved since registration

| # | Area Ref | Trigger Event | Trigger Class | Dimension | From (code + value) | To (code + value) | Permanence | Reversion Condition | Authority | Date |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [P.S.N.CCC.AA] | [event] | [T-class] | `B39-Lnn-DIM` | [old] | [new] | [Temporary / Provisional / Permanent] | [condition or "none"] | [Pre-registered map 11.6 / Check #21 ruling YYYY-MM-DD] | [YYYY-MM-DD] |

**Deferred Check #21 questions (Area 44.4)** — created by the first deferral. Each `[DEFER]` appends one row; a question's deferral count is the number of its rows. A Deferred row is not a drift entry: it moves no dimension and does not reset the D-3 count or clock.
| # | Area Ref | Character | Detection class | Evidence | Deferral | Date |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [P.S.N.CCC.AA] | [character] | [D-1 to D-4, or the T-class of an unmapped trigger] | [the line or event] | [deferral number] | [YYYY-MM-DD] |

**Superseded behaviors available for reversion (P39-10):**
| Code | Superseded value | Reverts under | Last seen |
|---|---|---|---|
```

*[L — updated at every executed drift event, whether pre-registered or Check #21-ruled. A D-3 `[KEEP PROFILE]` ruling appends a no-change entry (Dimension, From and To read `none`; Authority is the Check #21 ruling), which resets the D-3 count and clock to the ruling date (Area 44.4).]*

### 41.3 — Tier B and Tier C Storage

**Tier B** characters carry the full Section 11/12 content in their own file, `ledger-character-side/[name].md`, in the same seven-block order as Tier A (Area 41.1); their registry row in `ledger-character-side/_index.md` points at the file (Part 37 Threshold C-1).

**Tier C** characters carry a **one-paragraph behavioral line** in their `_index.md` functional row, covering the mandatory-core six and naming the single Signature and its origin. Format:

`**Behavioral (Part 39, core-6):** L01 [value]; L10 [value]; L03 [value]; L08 [value]; L29 [value]; L34 origin — [clause]. SIGNATURE: `B39-Lnn-DIM-nn` — [value].`

**Tier D** characters carry no Section 11 and no Section 12. Their code is stored in the Code cell of their registry row in `ledger-character-side/_index.md` (Section 3.5; Part 37 Area 2.11). On promotion to Tier C the behavioral line above may end with the words "Extra (Tier D code, Area P.S.N.CCC.AA):" followed by the conduct codes outside the core six, each with its value and the tag `[DERIVED]` (Rule 39.4.3.2a).

**Side villains and monsters.** A **Tier C side villain** carries the same one-paragraph behavioral line, in the Tier C table of `ledger-character-villain/_index.md`, and a **Tier D side villain** carries its code in the Code cell of its row in the Tier D table of the same `_index.md` (Part 18 Area 6.3). A **sentient monster** carries the full Section 11 and 12 content in its own file in `ledger-character-monster/`, in the same seven-block order as Tier A (Area 41.1). A **non-sentient monster** carries no Section 11 registration: its Part 40 trait lines, and for a Tier A or Tier B Individual its Signature Traits list in Section 11 of its file, reuse Part 39 dimensions by pointer and are not a Layer register (Part 40 Rule 40.5.1.2).

### 41.4 — The Collective Behavior Code Block

Part 37 Areas 2.4 (Location), 2.6 (Faction) and 2.7 (Civilization); a kingdom-state's code is stored in the Faction form (Area 2.6) on its ruling state's ledger. It is the storage for the codes that Layer L19 binds characters to.

```markdown
## Collective Behavior Code (Part 39 Layer L19) (B/L)

**Code Status:** [Registered YYYY-MM-DD | Not yet registered]
**Members currently bound:** [character cross-references]

**Shared conduct — what every member displays:**
- [conduct 1]
- [conduct 2]
- [conduct 3]

**Greeting and deference ritual:** [what members do on meeting, and how rank modulates it]
**In-group markers:** [what members do among themselves that outsiders do not]
**Conduct toward outsiders:** [the standard posture]
**Conduct when the group is criticized:** [the standard response]
**Induction conduct:** [what a new member is taught first, and by what means]
**Enforcement:** [how the code is corrected when a member departs from it, and by whom]

**For locations only — conduct the place imposes:**
- [what anyone standing here does differently, member or not]
- [what is forbidden here by custom rather than by rule]

**Registered deviations by member:** [character — seam, cross-referenced to their §11.5]

*[L — updated when membership, leadership or circumstance changes the code.]*
```

### 41.5 — Population Rule

**Rule 39.41.5.1.** Section 11 is populated in full at registration. Partial registration is allowed (this Rule and Rule 39.4.3.1 govern; the `/character-behavior` command follows them and does not narrow them). A partially populated Section 11 is a registration in progress; the character is drafted into an acting appearance once the author has set what the story needs (Rule 39.4.3.1). Section 12 is created empty at registration with the Drift Count and Immutable Core headers present.

**Rule 39.41.5.2 — The Owed Beats Record.** Section 11.3a lists the beats a registration owes the page. Each line reads: the rule, the code it concerns, and `Shown in Area P.S.N.CCC.AA` or `OPEN`. The rules that owe a beat are Rule 39.12.1.2 (unobserved conduct), Rule 39.15.1.1 (the joke not caught, for `L11-MECH-10`), Rule 39.17.1.1 (the mask in both registers, for `L13-MASK`), Rule 39.22.1.1 (the oath at its cost), the `B39-L19-SEAM-01` clause of Rule 39.23.1.2, and Rule 39.40.2.2 (the costly behavior). The block is opened with the registration, one `OPEN` line for each beat the character's codes owe (a character registered earlier gets the block at the first Post-Write Ledger Update that reads the file), and the Post-Write Ledger Update writes the Area reference at the Gate 2 of the Area that shows the beat. At each Section close the Continuity Checker's report adds a report-only line: the characters above Tier C who have a beat still `OPEN` after a full Section on the page, and each costly behavior still `OPEN` after the character's second Section on the page. The line is information for the author. It never fails an Area or blocks one, and no beat is added to a scene to clear it, because the confirmed spec chain and Part 30 decide what the story shows.

### 41.6 — Rollback When a Drift's Area Is Archived

**Rule 39.41.6.1 — Rollback Is the One Lawful Exception to Append-Only.** A drift belongs to the Area that caused it. When `/rewind-to`, `/redraft-area`, `/redraft-chapter`, `/reset-chapter` or `/reset-phase` archives or deletes that Area, the Continuity Checker's reconciliation pass undoes the drift, and undoes all of it:

1. **The Section 12 entry is kept and marked.** A drift row, or a Deferred Check #21 row, whose Area reference is among the archived or deleted Areas gets the note `rolled back YYYY-MM-DD` in its Date cell, with the name of the command that archived the Area. It is not deleted and not otherwise edited, so the log still shows that the drift once happened.
2. **A Permanent drift's prior value returns.** The Section 11 line the drift replaced is restored from the superseded-behaviors table, and that table's row gets the same `rolled back` note. A Temporary or Provisional drift changed no Section 11 line, so only step 1 applies to it. Where a later entry whose Area survives has moved the same dimension on (the Section 11 line no longer reads the rolled-back entry's To value), the prior value is not restored; the preview lists both entries and the author decides.
3. **A Drift Map write-back made by the drift's Check #21 ruling is marked the same way** (Section 11.6). A trigger whose map row is marked `rolled back` is an unmapped trigger when it next fires (Rule 39.42.5.2).
4. **The counters are read again** from the entries that are not rolled back: the Drift Count header, the D-3 count and clock, and a Deferred question's deferral count.

Each rollback is listed in the reconciliation preview, and nothing is written before the author confirms it (`/force-ledger-audit` Step 5). An entry whose Area survives is never rolled back by this rule.

**Rule 39.41.6.2 — Full Resets Clear Section 12.** `/hard-reset` and `/reset-story` leave no Area, so they clear Section 12 of every character ledger. For each Permanent drift the prior value is returned to Section 11 from the superseded-behaviors table first (Rule 39.41.6.1, step 2); then the log, the Deferred table and the superseded-behaviors table are emptied to their headers, and the Drift Count header returns to its registration text (Rule 39.41.5.1). A Section 11.6 map row written back by a ruling is marked `rolled back` as in step 3. `/reset-ledgers` leaves Section 12 as it is, because the Area it records is still on disk.

---

## Area 42 — The Conditional Drift Map

### 42.1 — Purpose

The Conditional Drift Map is the mechanism that satisfies P39-2 and P39-8 simultaneously: behavior is specified before the prose, *and* behavior changes as the story unfolds. It does this by registering the change in advance, conditional on a trigger, so that when the trigger fires the change is executed rather than invented. A character defeated in Chapter 22 does not have their new conduct decided in Chapter 22; it was decided at registration, and Chapter 22 executes it.

### 42.2 — The Trigger Taxonomy

| Class | Trigger | Typical source Part |
|---|---|---|
| **T-1 Combat outcome** | First kill; first defeat; first victory over a serious opponent; first mercy given; first mercy received; first failure to protect | Parts 5, 27 |
| **T-2 Loss** | Death of a mentor, ally, dependent, parent, child, partner | Parts 19, 26, 27 |
| **T-3 Betrayal** | Betrayed by a trusted party; committing a betrayal | Part 27 |
| **T-4 Power change** | Realm or tier crossing (a Part 3 Area 4.5 realm transition, or Mortal Tier 1 to Tier 2; a Level or sub-level step only where the drift map names it); affinity manifestation; Heart level change; power loss, sealing or suppression | Parts 3, 4, 31 |
| **T-5 Psychological** | PIS-3, PIS-5 and PIS-7 crossings; a terminal-state approach | Part 26 |
| **T-6 Consequence** | CSS-5 and CSS-8 crossings; vendetta formation; exile; public disgrace | Part 27 |
| **T-7 Ideological** | IE-3+ events; Ideological Rupture; blindspot revelation; conversion | Part 20 |
| **T-8 Age and time** | Age-band crossing; a named anniversary; elapsed-time thresholds within a Phase | Parts 16, 30 |
| **T-9 Relational** | Formation or ending of a bond; a dependency beginning or ending; a rivalry resolving | Parts 17, 19, 23 |
| **T-10 Positional** | Promotion, demotion, transfer, oath taken, oath released, faction change; a hero's or villain's arc-phase transition (Parts 17 and 18) | Parts 16, 17, 18, 22, 23 |
| **T-11 Revelation** | Learning the origin of one of their own behaviors (L34-KNOWN); learning a concealed fact about themselves | Parts 17, 24 |
| **T-12 Body** | Permanent injury; augmentation; chronic condition onset; recovery completed | Parts 8, 27 |

### 42.3 — Permanence Grades

| Grade | Definition | Section 12 handling |
|---|---|---|
| **Temporary** | Holds for the current Area; the baseline returns at the Area close without an event | Logged, with the return noted in the same entry |
| **Provisional** | Holds until a stated reversion condition occurs; may become Permanent if the condition never occurs within the Phase | Logged with the reversion condition; reviewed at Section close |
| **Permanent** | Replaces the registered value in Section 11; the prior value moves to the superseded table | Logged; Section 11 line edited; prior value preserved in Section 12 |

**Rule 39.42.3.1 — Permanence Is Declared at Registration, Not at Execution.** The map states the permanence grade for each mapped change in advance. Where the author wishes to decide at the moment, the map entry states `permanence: author-ruled at Check #21`, which converts the firing into a Check #21 enquiry rather than an automatic execution.

### 42.4 — Usual Map Entries

**Rule 39.42.4.1.** Every character above Tier C usually carries mapped entries, as the story foresees them, for:

1. Their first kill, or an explicit `[N-A]` with reason if they are incapable of combat.
2. Their first significant defeat.
3. Their first PIS-5 event.
4. The next realm or tier crossing their arc will reach (a hero maps every crossing on the Part 30 locked trajectory, per Part 37 Area 2.3).
5. The next age-band crossing, where one falls within the Phase (a hero maps every such crossing on the locked trajectory).
6. The loss of their most significant registered relationship.

A missing entry is shown at Check #20 as information, and an unmapped trigger is handled by Check #21 when it fires. These six are the usual entries: the high-yield triggers named in Rules 39.29.1.3 and 39.32.1.2 are mapped when the character's arc foresees them and otherwise fire the unmapped-trigger path (Rule 39.42.5.2).

### 42.5 — Execution

**Rule 39.42.5.1 — Automatic Execution.** When a mapped trigger fires in a drafted Area, the mapped change executes: the prose renders the new behavior, the Continuity Checker logs the entry to Section 12 at Gate 2, and Section 11 is edited if the grade is Permanent. No author consultation is required, and the pipeline does not pause, with one exception: a PIS-7 event, a CSS-8 event, an age-band crossing and a realm or tier crossing always open Check #21 (Area 44.2) even when the change is mapped. The mapped change is then the Continuity Checker's recommended ruling (Rule 39.44.4.1), and it executes on the author's answer.

**Rule 39.42.5.2 — Unmapped Trigger.** When a trigger fires that the map does not cover, Check #21 opens (Area 44) and the author rules. This is the gap-filling path, and every ruling made through it is written back into the map so that the same gap does not recur.

**Rule 39.42.5.3 — Coherence Check Against Provenance.** Before a Permanent change executes, it is checked against the affected behavior's `L34-END` value. A behavior whose registered ending condition is repudiation of an institution does not end because of a battlefield defeat; the mismatch is raised at Check #21 rather than executed.

**Rule 39.42.5.4 — Drift Count.** The number of dimensions moved in a Section (Rule 39.35.1.3) is shown with executed drift, mapped or ruled. A mapped change executes as mapped; the count never holds a change and never raises a Check #21 question.

**Rule 39.42.5.5 — Reversion Execution.** A superseded behavior listed in Section 12 reverts when its registered condition occurs. Reversion is logged as a new Section 12 entry, never as a deletion of the original.

### 42.6 — Worked Map Entry

| Trigger | Class | Dimension | From | To | Permanence | Reversion |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| First kill | T-1 | `L29-HANDS` | `-02` maintenance: cleans and checks a tool while waiting | `-06` nothing; the hands are held still by deliberate effort | Permanent | none |
| First kill | T-1 | `L05-FOOD` | `-01` fast, standing, finished before others sit | `-10` eats little and irregularly | Provisional | reverts when they next eat at a table with their own household |
| Realm crossing to Senior L1 | T-4 | `L26-PATIENCE` | `-02` falls with power | (no change; registered immutable) | — | — |
| Mentor death | T-2 | `L10-REFUSE` | `-05` cannot say no; agrees and then fails to perform | `-01` a flat no, reason offered only if asked | Permanent | none |

## Area 43 — Gate 1 Check #20: Behavioral Conformance Audit

### 43.1 — Status and Placement

**Check #20 is a blocking check in the Gate 1 sequence.** It is performed by the Continuity Checker after Check #19 and before the `[CONFIRM WRITE]` offer. A Gate 1 delivered with Check #20 FAILing is a Critical Violation (`guardrails/audit/guardrail-audit-gate-law.md`) and Gate 2 is blocked until a clean re-audit.

Check #20 asks one question: **does the drafted prose match the registered behavioral profiles of the characters in it?** The separate question of whether a profile should now *change* belongs to Check #21 and is never conflated with this one.

### 43.2 — The Countable Elements

**Read set.** Every count in this table that a rule sets per scene or per Area runs over the Area under audit (the Area window, defined in Part 2 Rule P2-Area-1); where a rule needs a whole scene that continues across consecutive Areas, it runs over the Areas of that scene read in order. A figure in this table is shown as information at every Gate 1; none of them is a FAIL. Each element keeps its own figure.

| Element | Test | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **20-1 Registration completeness** | Every character who acts or speaks in this Area holds a Part 39 registration (Rule 39.4.3.1); the element fails on a missing registration or a missing mandatory-core Layer (Rule 39.4.2.1), and a further unaddressed dimension is shown as information. **A Tier C person improvised in this Area** passes by the drafting agent's committed identification at Gate 1 (name, descriptor and tier) and by the registration written at Gate 2 (Rule 39.4.3.1, Route 2). **TIER D:** a **Named Conversational** character satisfies this element **for speech** by their `ledger-character-side/_index.md` registry row and by nothing else — at Gate 1 by the drafting agent's committed identification of them as Tier D, at Gate 2 by the row itself (Rule 39.4.3.1a); for a new person the identification includes the code and the element passes only when the code passes V1 to V9 (Rule 39.3.5.7), the code itself never printed in chat; for a returning person it passes when the identification matches the row (V8). They satisfy it **for speech only**: a Tier D character rendered making a consequential choice, driving a beat, or carrying a story-state decision **FAILS this element**; the remedy is that the character is FLAGGED TO THE AUTHOR and the author rules, promote to Tier C or keep them at Tier D and let the moment stand. The auditor never promotes, never declines to promote, and never widens the tier. The FAIL is cleared by the author's ruling, and an open Tier D consequence flag blocks Gate 2 exactly as a FAIL does. **Recurrence is NOT a finding at this element**: a Tier D character appearing in a later Area is correctly registered by their existing row and is not scored against here | PASS / FAIL — name every unregistered actor, **and name every Tier D character rendered acting beyond speech, with the flag raised rather than a remedy applied** |
| **20-2 Signature conformance** | No rendered behavior contradicts a registered Signature without a Section 12 drift entry authorizing it. **Tier D, light form:** no rendered behavior contradicts a code of the row's Code cell or its `[SIG]` code (Rule 39.3.5.7 V9); a finding is cleared by revising the prose or by the author's word, never by a drift entry | PASS / FAIL — name the code, the line, and the contradiction |
| **20-3 Retired** | The Layer L35 swap test is retired; the number is kept so the other elements do not renumber | Not scored |
| **20-4 Signature recurrence** | Over the last five Areas in which each character appeared, in how many of those five Areas each Signature fired (Rule 39.3.3.4). The five Areas are read from the Signatures-fired records of the last five as-built Area blocks of the chapter spec (Part 38 Rule 3.6.1 item 8), with the Area under audit added | Area count shown per Signature (information only) |
| **20-5 Contradiction and cost presence** | Where the Area gives occasion, a registered contradiction or costly behavior is rendered rather than smoothed over. Shown as information (never a FAIL; no beat is added to meet it) | Shown (information only) |
| **20-6 Waiting beat (L29)** | In each Area, whether each named character present but not currently acting has a rendered idle beat from their L29 registration (Rule 39.33.1.2); a character present for under half the Area, or one of four or more silent members, may share one collective waiting beat | Shown with the character's name (information only) |
| **20-7 Off-register count** | The marked and unmarked off-register acts per character in the Section (Rule 39.40.2.4) | Count shown (information only; an unmarked act is a conformance question) |
| **20-8 Collective code conformance** | Where members of one collective are present, whether the code shows (Rule 39.23.1.3; three or more is the usual case); where a superior member and a subordinate member are both present, the subordinate's rank modulation shows before any dialogue acknowledges the arrival (Rule 39.23.1.4); each member's registered seam is respected (Rule 39.23.1.2) | PASS / FAIL |
| **20-9 Demonstrated-not-named** | No sentence states a Part 39 registration as a narrator-asserted property (Rule 39.40.2.5) | PASS / FAIL — quote the sentence |
| **20-10 Provenance integrity** | No newly rendered habitual behavior appears that is absent from the register; a genuinely new habit is a Check #21 matter, not a free invention | PASS / FAIL |
| **20-11 Age-band anchors (L30)** | In each Area, every YC-1 or YC-2 character present renders at least one registered child-anchor (Rule 39.34.1.1) | PASS / FAIL / N-A |
| **20-12 Boundary integrity** | No Part 39 registration has been rendered in a way that breaches Part 5, Part 6, Part 3, Part 29 formatting, or any other Part's technical requirement (P39-14) | PASS / FAIL — name the Part and the rule |

**Monsters.** Part 40 Rule 40.5.4.1 owns this reading. Element 20-2 is read in light form for a non-sentient monster: no rendered behavior contradicts a recorded Baseline trait of its Kind or a Living trait of its Individual (Part 40 Area 5). Element 20-1 does not apply to a non-sentient monster, which is not a character, and a sentient monster is registered in full and read by every element. No element is added, and the count of 22 checks is unchanged.

### 43.3 — The Threshold-Consultation Interface

Elements 20-4, 20-5, 20-6 and 20-7, the three-or-more test of 20-8 and the Tier D conduct-code guide post (V7, Rule 39.3.5.7) are the counts of Check #20; this is the single list of them (the gate law and the behavioral-authenticity guardrail point here). **A count is shown with its computed value and the line it crosses as information; it is not a FAIL, it raises no question, and it is never auto-revised.** The remaining elements follow the standard Correction Protocol, except where a remedy would change what happens on the page rather than how a line is worded, which is likewise brought to the author.

### 43.4 — Report Format

```
CHECK #20 — BEHAVIORAL CONFORMANCE (Part 39)
Characters in Area: [names, with tier and registration status]
20-1 Registration completeness ....... PASS
20-2 Signature conformance ........... FAIL — [Character A] `B39-L15-BLAME-01` (takes blame publicly): line 47 has
                                         [Character A] assigning blame in open court. No Section 12 entry authorizes this.
20-3 (retired) ....................... not scored
20-4 Signature recurrence ............ shown — [Character A] 3/5 Areas, [Character B] 2/5 Areas, [Character C] 1/5 Areas (information)
20-5 Contradiction and cost .......... shown (information)
20-6 Waiting beat .................... NOTE — [Character B] present in beats 3-6, no L29 beat rendered (information)
20-7 Off-register count .............. shown — 0 this Area, 0 this Section (information)
20-8 Collective code conformance ..... PASS
20-9 Demonstrated-not-named .......... PASS
20-10 Provenance integrity ........... PASS
20-11 Age-band anchors ............... N/A — no YC-1 or YC-2 character present
20-12 Boundary integrity ............. PASS
RESULT: FAIL (1 element) — Gate 2 blocked pending clean re-audit.
```
