## Area 44 — Gate 1 Check #21: Behavioral Change Detection

### 44.1 — Status, Purpose and the Distinction From Check #20

**Check #21 does not run for a Tier D person** (Rule 39.3.5.6 item 5). **Check #21 is a blocking check** performed immediately after Check #20. It blocks by *asking*, not by failing: where it detects a condition, it raises an `AskUserQuestion` and Gate 2 is held until the author rules. Where it detects nothing, it reports SILENT and the pipeline proceeds without interruption.

Check #20 polices drift-as-error. Check #21 surfaces drift-as-growth. The same drafted line — a character behaving against their registration — may be either, and only the author can say which. Check #20 reports it as a conformance question; Check #21 asks whether the profile itself should now move. **A single line may legitimately appear in both checks**, and where it does, the Check #21 ruling governs: an author ruling of `[EVOLVE]` retrospectively clears the Check #20 FAIL for that line.

**Purpose:** this Check exists because a nine-year Phase must be allowed to change people. A registry that only enforced consistency would produce characters who are coherent and dead. Check #21 is the mechanism that keeps the profile living.

**Widened by Part 43 (Character Evolution Law, 2026-10-06).** Check #21 also detects evolution for every character, all tiers, not only the characters that carry a full Layer register: it asks whether anything recorded about the person has changed (behaviour, fighting style, speech, morality, psychology, relationships, routines; Part 43 Area 3). Who rules: for a hero and for a Tier A side character, villain or monster the checker raises the `AskUserQuestion` and the author rules; for a Tier B, C or D character the checker decides at Gate 1 and records its decision and its reason, and asks no question; a change to a registered immutable (any tier) is always put to the author, and an agent never changes an immutable on its own. Gate 2 presents every evolution of the Area, all tiers, with what changed, the layer, the Area, who ruled and the reason. A `[EVOLVE]` ruling writes a dated Evolution Entry into Section 14 of the ledger (Part 37 Area 2.1) and leaves the registered profile as the base; it does not count as drift (Part 43 Rule 43.6.2.1). For a Tier D person the Rule 39.3.5.6 item 5 line above still holds for the Layer questions of this Part: no Layer question is raised, and any evolution of a Tier D code is ruled by the checker under Part 43 Rule 43.2.1.5. The four detection classes and the five-way response set below are unchanged. The full law is Part 43.

### 44.2 — Firing Cadence

Check #21 runs on every Area and *fires* — raises a question — only under the conditions below. On all other Areas it reports SILENT.

| Condition | Fires |
|---|---|
| **Event-triggered** | An unmapped T-1 through T-12 trigger (Area 42.2) occurs in the Area, or a mapped one that breaks L34-END (Rule 39.42.5.3) |
| **Contradiction-triggered** | The prose renders behavior contradicting a registered Signature (the Check #20 element 20-2 finding) |
| **Flag-triggered** | A ledger item marked "flagged for author confirmation" (or worded to the same effect) is not yet ruled: it is raised once at the first Area where the character speaks or acts, then at each Area close until the author rules. This firing is separate from the boundary reviews |
| **Boundary review — Area close** | At the close of an Area in which the prose shows a POV character's profile moving |
| **Boundary review — Section close** | At the close of a Section, for characters whose profiles the prose shows moving; run by `/next-section` before the next Section opens, where no Gate 1 or Gate 2 exists |
| **Automatic** | Any PIS-7 event (Area 31.2, `L27-PIS7`), any CSS-8 event, any age-band crossing, any realm or tier crossing |

A boundary review that finds none of D-1 to D-4 and no unmapped trigger reports SILENT with the D-3 count and asks nothing.

**Pending author confirmations.** Each character index (`ledger-character-hero`, `ledger-character-side` and `ledger-character-villain`) carries a "Pending author confirmations" table with the columns character, item, file and section, and what it blocks. The Post-Write Ledger Update adds a row when it finds or writes an item marked "flagged for author confirmation" and removes the row when the author rules, so the table is the single list to rule from. The Flag-triggered row above raises the item; the table shows what is still open. `/status` lists the table.

### 44.3 — The Four Detection Classes

| Class | What it detects | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| **D-1 Signature contradiction** | The prose shows the character behaving against a registered Signature | Either a craft error to correct or the first sign of real change. The author rules which; the checker never assumes. |
| **D-2 Unmapped trigger** | A governed trigger fired that the Conditional Drift Map does not cover | The map has a gap. The ruling fills it (Rule 39.42.5.2). |
| **D-3 Pressure without change** | The prose shows a person who has been through events that should have moved them and has not | The subtler failure of a long arc. It is a story judgement, not a count (P39-8). |
| **D-4 Band or tier crossing** | An age-band crossing (L30) or a realm/tier crossing (L26) has occurred | Both expect a registered consequence or a recorded no-change by their own Layer rules (Rules 39.34.1.3, 39.30.1.1). |

**D-3 is a story judgement.** The Continuity Checker raises it when the prose shows a person who has been through events that should have moved them and has not. No count of events and no elapsed time fires it. A D-3 count and clock, where kept, are information only and never fire D-3. A `[KEEP PROFILE]` ruling on a D-3 firing is not raised again on the same events, only on events after the ruling date (Area 44.4).

### 44.4 — The Author Response Set

When Check #21 fires, the Continuity Checker raises an `AskUserQuestion` presenting the character, the detection class, the evidence, the affected dimensions, and these five options:

| Token | Effect |
|---|---|
| **`[KEEP PROFILE]`** | The registration stands. The prose is corrected to match it. Any Check #20 element 20-2 FAIL on the same line remains a FAIL and must be revised. A `[KEEP PROFILE]` ruling on a D-3 firing also appends a Section 12 no-change entry, which resets the D-3 count and clock to the ruling date; Section 12 stays append-only. |
| **`[EVOLVE]`** | The registration changes permanently. Under Part 43 (2026-10-06) the change is written as a dated Evolution Entry in Section 14 and the registered Section 11 value stays as the base; where a Layer value is moved as before, the prior value moves to the Section 12 superseded table, and a Section 12 entry is written naming the trigger. Any related Check #20 FAIL is cleared. The Conditional Drift Map is updated so the same trigger is mapped in future. |
| **`[REVISE AREA]`** | The prose was wrong in a way that goes beyond wording. The beat is redrafted. Distinct from `[KEEP PROFILE]` in that it acknowledges the drafted event itself, not merely its rendering, was mistaken. |
| **`[PROVISIONAL]`** | The change is real but temporary. A Section 12 entry is written at Provisional grade with a stated reversion condition. Section 11 is *not* edited. This is the option that prevents a stress reaction from being mistaken for permanent growth. |
| **`[DEFER]`** | The question carries to the next boundary review. The Area proceeds. Each deferral appends one row to the Deferred table of Section 12 (character, detection class, evidence, and its deferral number), written by the ledger instance on `[CONFIRM WRITE]`; the count of a question is the number of its rows, which keeps Section 12 append-only. Deferred questions are presented together at Area or Section close; a deferred question stays open until the author rules, and no number of deferrals forces a ruling. |

**Rule 39.44.4.1 — A Proposed Ruling Accompanies Every Question.** The Continuity Checker states its own recommended option with reasoning drawn from the character's ideology (Part 20), psychology (Part 26), arc phase (Parts 17/18/19) and L31 drift parameters. The author is confirming or overriding a considered proposal, never deciding cold. The question opens with one plain sentence that says what the Area did and what the profile says; the codes (detection class, Layer code, response token) follow it and never replace it (`quality/guardrail-plain-language.md`, which carries a worked example).

**Rule 39.44.4.2 — Rulings Are Written Back.** Every `[EVOLVE]` and `[PROVISIONAL]` ruling updates the Conditional Drift Map (Section 11.6) as well as the log at Gate 2, after `[CONFIRM WRITE]` (a `[SKIP WRITE]` discards the ruling with the draft); a ruling on a Section-close review is made at `/next-section`, where no Gate 2 exists, and is written at once by a continuity-checker ledger instance, so that the map grows more complete as the story runs and D-2 firings become rarer.

### 44.5 — Report Format

```
CHECK #21 — BEHAVIORAL CHANGE DETECTION (Part 39)
Cadence: event-triggered (T-1 first mercy given, unmapped) + Area close
FIRING for: [Character A]

D-1 Signature contradiction: `B39-L29-HANDS-02` (finds a task for the hands while waiting).
    Line 112 renders the hands still and empty through the whole of the wait.
D-2 Unmapped trigger: first mercy given (T-1) — no row in the Conditional Drift Map.
D-3 Pressure without change: none shown.
D-4 Crossing: none this Area.

NO MAPPED CHANGE: Section 11.6 has no row for first mercy given. The drafted line renders
`L29-HANDS-02` becoming `-06`.

CHECKER RECOMMENDATION: [EVOLVE] — the contradiction is the character changing at an unmapped
trigger. Recommend logging to Section 12, editing Section 11.1 and adding a first-mercy-given row
to Section 11.6 (Permanent) so the gap does not recur (Rule 39.42.5.2).

Author ruling required: [KEEP PROFILE] / [EVOLVE] / [REVISE AREA] / [PROVISIONAL] / [DEFER]
```

---

## Area 45 — The Behavioral Interview Protocol

### 45.1 — Purpose and Method

The Behavioral Interview is how a character's Section 11 is populated. It runs through `AskUserQuestion` and is the Plan Mode phase of the `/character-behavior` command under `guardrails/commands/guardrail-plan-then-auto-execution.md`: every author-facing question is asked and resolved before the write begins.

**Rule 39.45.1.1 — Derived-Default Method.** The interview does not ask all 291 dimensions. It asks the load-bearing ones and derives the rest from named canon evidence, presenting the derivations to the author for override before the write. The split is:

| Population route | Approximate count | Method |
|---|---|---|
| **ASKED** | 40–50 dimensions | The 12+ Signatures, one defining dimension per Layer, and every dimension where existing canon gives no evidence either way. Delivered as roughly 10–13 `AskUserQuestion` calls of up to four questions each. This is the full route, used for a Hero, a Villain and a Side Tier A character; Tier B and Tier C take the lighter routes of Rule 39.45.1.4. |
| **DERIVED** | ~240 dimensions | Inferred from the character's existing ledger sections, registered prosodic signatures, ideology, psychology, rank, culture, relationships and arc, with the evidence cited inline on every line. |
| **N-A** | variable | Marked with a stated reason. |

**Rule 39.45.1.2 — Every Derivation Cites Its Evidence.** A `[DERIVED]` line without an inline evidence citation is a defect (Rule 39.3.3.1). Acceptable evidence is: a named ledger section of this or another entity, an established prose line, a registered prosodic signature, or a named canon Part rule that constrains the value. "Seems likely" is not evidence.

**Rule 39.45.1.3 — Derived Values Are Reviewed Before They Are Written.** After the asked phase, the full derived set is presented to the author as a per-character digest grouped by Layer, each line with its evidence. The author overrides in plain language. Only after the digest is accepted is the ledger written.

**Rule 39.45.1.4 — Lighter Routes for Tier B and Tier C.** The registration written is the same at every tier: Tier B still addresses all thirty-six Layers with twelve Signatures, two contradiction pairs and a costly behavior (Rule 39.4.1.1), and Tier C still registers the mandatory-core six with one Signature (Rule 39.4.2.1). What the lighter routes change is how much of that the author is asked and how much is derived under Rules 39.45.1.2 and 39.45.1.3. The figures below say how large an interview usually is. They are information only, and no step fails or stops because an interview runs longer or shorter.
1. **Tier B.** Ask only the twelve Signatures (each with its Layer L34 origin), the two contradiction pairs and the costly behavior, usually about 16 dimensions in about four `AskUserQuestion` calls. Derive every other dimension, the six usual drift entries included, each with its evidence (Rule 39.45.1.2). A derived line that has no evidence is not guessed: it is marked `ASK` in the digest and the author answers it there. Show the derived set as one digest batched for the whole scene cast, each character's block standing alone (Rules 39.4.3.5 and 39.45.3.4).
2. **Tier C.** Ground the character (Step 1), derive the mandatory-core six with evidence, and ask only the one Signature and its origin (Layer L34), usually in one or two calls. The six derived lines are shown in the same round, and nothing is written until they are accepted (Rule 39.45.1.3).
3. **Hero, Villain and Side Tier A** use the full route of Rule 39.45.1.1.

### 45.2 — The Nine-Step Registration Sequence (eight live steps plus the retired Step 6)

| Step | Action |
|---|---|
| **1. Ground** | Read the character's full existing ledger file, their `_index.md` row, every ledger that cross-references them, and their registered prosodic signatures. Read the collective codes of every faction, civilization and location they are bound to. |
| **2. Derive** | Produce a provisional value for every one of the 291 dimensions, each tagged `[DERIVED]` with evidence, or `[N-A]` with reason, or flagged `ASK` where evidence is absent or the dimension is load-bearing. |
| **3. Ask — identity core** | First `AskUserQuestion` round: the Signature candidates, the immutable core (L31), the contradiction pairs and the costly behavior (L36). These four determine everything downstream and are asked first. |
| **4. Ask — layer defining** | Subsequent rounds: one defining dimension per Layer not already settled, plus every `ASK`-flagged dimension. |
| **5. Ask — drift** | The Conditional Drift Map: the six usual entries (Rule 39.42.4.1) plus any arc-specific triggers the character's registered arc will reach. |
| **6. (retired)** | Layer L35 is retired: no registration is checked against another, and no exchange or named axis is recorded. Its three dimensions are written `[N-A]` citing the retirement of L35 (Area 39) and still count toward completeness: 36/36 Layers and 291/291 dimensions addressed, 288 of them live. |
| **7. Digest** | Present the full derived set to the author, grouped by Layer, with evidence. Accept plain-language overrides. |
| **8. Write** | Write Section 11 and create Section 12. Update the character's `_index.md` row with registration status. Update any collective code's member list. |
| **9. Verify** | Show the tier guides: Layer count, Signature count, Signature spread across Layers, contradiction pairs, costly behavior, drift entries; a short count is information only. Then sweep the named overlapping pairs for coherence: L08-LINE with L18-WEIGHT, L08-UNSEEN with L13-MASK, L09-CAPACITY with L09-MECH and L09-TELL, and L11-WITH with L14-DELTA. In each pair the two chosen values agree, or the disagreement is registered as a contradiction pair (Rule 39.40.2.1). |

Tier B and Tier C run the same nine steps, with the asked rounds of Steps 3 to 5 reduced as Rule 39.45.1.4 sets out. Steps 7 to 9 are unchanged.

### 45.3 — Question Construction Standards

**Rule 39.45.3.1 — Options Are Concrete Behaviors, Never Categories.** A question offering *disciplined / relaxed / severe* has offered categories and violates P39-3. Options are drawn from the coded catalog, quoted in full, with the code shown.

**Rule 39.45.3.2 — The Recommended Option Is First and Reasoned.** Where derivation produces a favored value, it is presented first with its evidence stated in the option description, so the author is confirming a reasoned proposal.

**Rule 39.45.3.3 — Author-Specified Is Always Reachable.** Every question's option set is accompanied by the `-99` AUTHOR-SPECIFIED route. The interview never forces a catalog option.

**Rule 39.45.3.4 — Ensemble Order.** Where several unregistered characters share scenes, they are interviewed in descending tier order (Rule 39.4.3.5), each standing alone.

### 45.4 — Re-Interview

**Rule 39.45.4.1.** A registered character is re-interviewed only on tier promotion (Rule 39.4.3.2), on the opening of a previously closed Layer (for example L17 when an age gate lifts), or on author instruction. Ordinary change flows through Area 42 and Check #21, never through re-interview.

---

## Area 46 — Part 38 Chapter Spec Behavioral Plan Interface

### 46.1 — The Behavioral Plan Block

**Schema authority note.** Part 38 is the authority for chapter-spec structure and lifecycle. Area 46 supplies the block's content model; on any structural disagreement, Part 38 governs.

Each per-Area block in a chapter spec gains a Behavioral Plan:

```markdown
#### Behavioral Plan (Part 39)

**Characters appearing:** [name — tier — registration status]
**Active collective code(s):** [faction / location code, cross-referenced]
**Signatures expected to fire:**
| Character | Signature code | In which beat | Repetition position (information) | Fired in Area (written at Gate 2) |
|---|---|---|---|---|

**Waiting beats planned (L29):** [character — beat]
**Contradiction or cost rendered:** [character — which, and where]
**Transmission rendered (L16):** [caregiver to dependent — behavior — where | none]
**Anticipated drift:**
| Character | Trigger | Class | Mapped? | Expected dimension change | Permanence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

**Off-register act planned:** [character — act — how it is marked | none]
```

### 46.2 — Rules

**Rule 39.46.2.1 — Behavior Is Planned With the Beats.** The Behavioral Plan is built during `/chapter-spec` alongside the beat plan, not appended afterward. This is the operational form of P39-2: conduct is settled before the prose the same way events are.

**Rule 39.46.2.2 — Check #18 Covers the Behavioral Plan.** Part 38's Gate 1 Check #18 (Spec Conformance) tests the drafted Area against its planned spec slot. That test includes the Behavioral Plan: a Signature planned to fire and absent, or an unplanned drift executed, is a Check #18 variance and is flagged for the Gate 2 as-built sync exactly as any other variance is. A Signature, waiting beat or cost that moves or is absent is a listed, non-blocking difference, accepted by `[CONFIRM WRITE]` and updated in the spec at Gate 2. An unplanned drift and an unregistered actor are core differences.

**Rule 39.46.2.3 — Unregistered Characters Block the Plan.** A chapter spec that plans an acting appearance for a character with no tier-compliant registration records that fact as a blocking item; the registration is completed before the prose is drafted (Rule 39.4.3.1).

> **TIER D.** The rule above applies in full to every registered tier. The one distinction Tier D introduces is the tier's own boundary rather than an exception to it:
>
> - **A spec that plans a Tier D character to SPEAK is NOT a blocking item.** Named Conversational characters exist precisely so a planned conversation can have two sides, and their registration is a Gate 2 registry row (Rule 39.4.3.1a). A spec need not name them at all — they are ordinarily introduced by the drafting agent inside the Area — and where a spec does name one, it records them as Tier D and moves on. **Nor is a spec that plans a RETURNING Tier D character a blocking item**: recurrence promotes nothing, the existing registry row still registers them, and the spec records them as Tier D and moves on exactly as for a first appearance.
> - **A spec that plans a Tier D character to ACT CONSEQUENTIALLY IS a blocking item, exactly as before.** A planned choice, a planned beat, a planned story-state decision is outside the tier, **and the block is recorded and cleared by AN AUTHOR RULING ON THE FLAG, raised at spec build through the Part 28 Area 19 Decision Consultation Gate (Rule 39.46.2.5). The author rules promote-to-Tier-C or keep-at-Tier-D. The Outline Planner never decides it, never pre-empts it by promoting into the spec, and never clears it by re-labelling the act as conversation.**
>
> **The test at spec time is the test at draft time** (Rule 39.4.3.1a): *does the story have to remember what they did?* **The Part 38 side of this reconciliation is at `spec-story-specification-law` Rule 38.2.2a.1**, which is written to the same effect; the two agree and neither is to be read as narrowing the other. Part 19 Rule 19.1.2.4 owns the promotion question and Rule 38.2.2a.1 restates it rather than owning it.

**Rule 39.46.2.4 — Anticipated Drift Is Not Settled Canon.** A drift entry in a chapter spec is a plan, not an establishment. Part 38's authority model holds: the ledger supersedes the spec, and a drift becomes canon only when it executes and is logged to Section 12 at Gate 2.

**Rule 39.46.2.5 — The Decision Consultation Gate Carries Behavioral Questions.** Where the Behavioral Plan cannot be completed without an author decision — an unmapped trigger the chapter will reach, a Layer that must open — the question is raised in the Part 28 Area 19 Decision Consultation Gate during spec build rather than surfacing mid-draft.

---

## Area 47 — Narration Standards N39-1 through N39-16

**N39-1 — Behavior arrives inside other work.** A registered behavior is rendered while the character is doing something else. A sentence whose only function is to display a habit is a catalog entry with punctuation.

**N39-2 — The reader assembles the pattern; the narrator never states it.** Across scenes the reader learns that this person squares objects. No sentence ever says that they square objects as a matter of habit. Co-extensive with Rule 39.40.2.5, Part 36 Area 17 and Part 28 Area 13.

**N39-3 — One behavior per beat.** A beat that renders three registered dimensions at once reads as a demonstration. Behavior is distributed across the scene, not stacked.

**N39-4 — The body carries hierarchy before the dialogue does.** Rank, deference and authority reach the page as position, timing and object handling first (Rule 39.17.1.2).

**N39-5 — Non-speaking characters are doing something.** Apply Rule 39.33.1.2; the beat may come from L29 or L10-LISTEN.

**N39-6 — Context modulation is shown, not explained.** When a superior enters and a subordinate's conduct changes, the change is rendered and its cause is left to the reader.

**N39-7 — A collective code shows in the group before it shows in any member.** Where members of one collective appear, render the shared conduct before an individual's deviation from it, as a craft guide (Rule 39.23.1.3).

**N39-8 — The seam is where the character lives.** The most valuable single beat available in a group scene is the moment one member's registered deviation from their code becomes visible. Plan it; do not stumble into it.

**N39-9 — Drift is rendered as an absence first.** A behavior that has changed is most powerfully shown by the reader noticing that the old one did not happen. The new behavior may follow; the absence comes first.

**N39-10 — A reverted behavior is never explained on its return.** When a superseded behavior resurfaces under its registered condition (P39-10), the prose renders it and says nothing. The reader who has been paying attention supplies the whole weight of it.

**N39-11 — Child conduct survives prodigy status.** A YC-1 or YC-2 character renders at least one registered child-anchor per Area in which they appear, regardless of their realm, talent, rank, or the gravity of the scene (Rule 39.34.1.1).

**N39-12 — Villain conduct is coherent from inside.** A villain's L03, L08, L27 and L28 registrations are constrained by their Part 18 psychological archetype and must render as internally consistent conduct rather than as menace. The Armored, the Consumed, the Calcified, the Fractured and the Hollow each *wait* differently, *eat* differently and *correct a subordinate* differently, and those differences are where the archetype becomes visible.

**N39-13 — The costly behavior is paid on the page.** A registered cost that is never incurred is a claim. Over an arc, the character is usually shown losing something specific and named because of a registered behavior (Rule 39.40.2.2); the confirmed Chapter Spec decides when.

**N39-14 — Off-register acts are marked.** When a character acts out of character, someone notices — another character, or the character themselves. An unmarked off-register act reads as an authorial error even when it is deliberate (Rule 39.40.2.4).

**N39-15 — Do not render the code.** A catalog option's wording is a specification, not prose. Two characters holding the same option code produce different sentences, because the option is combined with their provenance, their context and their other Layers (Rule 39.3.2.4). Copying catalog wording into prose is a Part 36 vitality failure.

**N39-16 — Behavior never buys an exemption.** No registered behavior renders in a way that breaches a technical requirement of another Part. A character registered as reckless does not thereby exceed a Part 5 escalation ceiling; a character registered as tireless does not thereby exceed a Part 3 energy reserve (P39-14, Check #20 element 20-12).

---

## Area 48 — Unregistered Behavioral Dimensions

### 48.1 — Purpose

The thirty-six Layers and 291 dimensions were authored to be exhaustive and are not thereby complete. A story running for multiple Phases will reach conduct that fits no existing dimension well. Forcing such conduct into the nearest available dimension corrupts the register: the value stops describing the character and starts describing the catalog's limits. Area 48 is the governed alternative.

### 48.2 — The Flagging Protocol

**Rule 39.48.2.1 — Flag, Do Not Force.** Where a drafting agent or the author identifies conduct that no existing dimension governs, it is flagged rather than assigned. The flag format is:

`⚠ UNREGISTERED BEHAVIORAL DIMENSION — [character]: [the conduct, in one clause]. Nearest existing dimension: [code] — rejected because [reason]. Proposed Layer: [Lnn]. Author direction requested.`

**Rule 39.48.2.2 — Interim Recording.** Pending the author's direction, the conduct is recorded in the character's Section 11 under a provisional identifier `B39-Lnn-PENDING-[short-slug]` with the full description in free text and the flag date. A provisional identifier is valid canon for the character and is not valid for citation by any other character until the dimension is registered.

**Rule 39.48.2.3 — Three Outcomes.** The author rules one of:

| Ruling | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Assign** | The conduct does fit an existing dimension; the provisional entry is replaced by the code and the flag is closed. |
| **Extend** | A new option is added to an existing dimension, taking the next free number. The provisional entry is replaced by the new code. A one-line note is added to that Layer's Area. |
| **Register** | A new dimension is created within an existing Layer, with its own option catalog of at least six entries plus `-99`. This is a Part 39 Amendment Protocol action (Area 49) and updates the dimension count. |

**Rule 39.48.2.4 — A New Layer Is a Different Matter.** Conduct that fits no existing *Layer* — not merely no dimension — is escalated as a full Part 39 amendment under Area 49 and is never created by the flagging protocol. Thirty-six Layers is a considered structure; a thirty-seventh is an author decision, not a drafting convenience.

**Rule 39.48.2.5 — Flags Do Not Block.** An unregistered-dimension flag is not a Gate 1 failure. Prose containing flagged conduct may pass Check #20 provided the conduct is recorded under Rule 39.48.2.2 and the flag is open. Flags left open across three Sections are reported at the next Section-close boundary review.

---

## Area 49 — Part 39 Amendment Protocol

**Rule 39.49.1 — Amendment Classes.**

| Class | Examples | Approval |
|---|---|---|
| **A-1 Option addition** | A new option appended to an existing dimension | Author confirmation; no version increment |
| **A-2 Option deprecation** | An option marked `[DEPRECATED]`, retained in place | Author confirmation; ledgers citing it are surveyed and migrated |
| **A-3 Dimension creation** | A new dimension inside an existing Layer, with a catalog of at least six options plus `-99` | Author confirmation; dimension count updated; minor version increment |
| **A-4 Layer creation or removal** | A thirty-seventh Layer, or the retirement of one | Formal Canon Amendment; Area renumbering; major version increment; all registered characters surveyed |
| **A-5 Rule amendment** | Any change to a numbered rule, tier requirement, countable threshold, or check element | Formal Canon Amendment; guardrail and agent updates propagated |
| **A-6 Boundary amendment** | Any change to Area 2's sovereignty table | Formal Canon Amendment; the affected Part is amended in the same pass, never unilaterally |

**Rule 39.49.2 — Codes Are Never Reassigned.** Under every class, a published option code retains its meaning permanently (Rule 39.3.2.2).

**Rule 39.49.3 — Propagation Checklist.** Every A-3 amendment or higher propagates to: this skill file; `canon-manifest/skill-index.json` (area list, counts); `canon-manifest/domain-map.json` and `dependency-graph.json` where dependencies change; `guardrails/quality/guardrail-behavioral-authenticity.md`; `guardrails/audit/guardrail-audit-gate-law.md` where a check element changes; the four agent definitions; `commands/character-behavior.md`; and Part 37 Area 2 where the ledger schema changes. An amendment landed in this file alone is incomplete. Every A-1 or A-2 amendment updates the Appendix B count and the Area header of its dimension in the same pass.

**Rule 39.49.4 — Registered Characters Are Surveyed, Never Silently Migrated.** Where an amendment invalidates an existing registration, every affected character is listed and the author rules on each. No registered behavior is rewritten on the system's own judgment.

**Rule 39.49.5 — Amendments Rewrite In Place.** An amendment rewrites the affected rule text in place. The file states current law only and carries no change log.

| **Part 38** | HARD | Chapter spec structure, lifecycle, Check #18, the spec authority model | The Behavioral Plan block's content model |
| **Part 40** | SOFT | The monster definition, the twelve monster traits, the four monster depth tiers, the sentient reserve and the villain tiers of Part 18 Area 6.3 | The dimensions the twelve traits reuse (by pointer), the Check #20 light reading of monster traits, the full registration of a sentient monster, and the Tier C and Tier D rows for side villains (Area 4.1, Area 41.3) |

# Appendix A — Worked Example Profiles

> **These six profiles are non-canon.** They describe invented characters who exist nowhere in the Starward Saga and are present solely to show what a completed registration looks like. They may be edited freely without touching canon and must never be mistaken for a registered character's profile. A.1 is complete in form; A.2 to A.6 show Signature sets and sample entries only. A real registration additionally carries all 291 Layer Register lines.

## A.1 — Archetype: The Child Prodigy

**Example character:** Ilsa Renn, age nine, exceptional cultivation talent, daughter of a provincial administrator.

**Signature Set (12):**

| # | Code | Behavior | Origin (L34) | Immutable |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `B39-L01-HANDS-06` | Counts something on the joints of her fingers when made to wait | L34-CLASS-01 institutional: the counting drill of her first cultivation tutor | Yes |
| 2 | `B39-L29-TOLER-02` | Cannot be idle; idleness is a physical distress | L34-CLASS-05 event: the six weeks she was confined after her first qi surge | Yes |
| 3 | `B39-L30-ANCHOR-04` | Asks the naive unanswerable question at the worst moment | L34-CLASS-08 cultural: nobody has yet taught her not to | No |
| 4 | `B39-L30-ANCHOR-03` | Hunger and tiredness arrive suddenly and completely and override everything | L34-CLASS-07 body: she is nine | No |
| 5 | `B39-L30-ANCHOR-07` | Attachment to a chipped counting-stone of no value | L34-CLASS-02 person: her tutor gave it to her | No |
| 6 | `B39-L06-INFO-01` | Asks direct questions and keeps asking until the answer is exact | L34-CLASS-09 self-constructed: she decided at seven that adults lie by stopping early | No |
| 7 | `B39-L02-FIRST-03` | Registers the condition of a building before its occupants | L34-CLASS-03 absorbed: her father surveys rooms and she has copied it unknowing | No |
| 8 | `B39-L24-SESSION-04` | Practises before dawn, alone, non-negotiably | L34-CLASS-01 institutional | No |
| 9 | `B39-L03-MASK-04` | Shame presents as formality; her register climbs when she has done wrong | L34-CLASS-03 absorbed from her father | No |
| 10 | `B39-L10-INTERRUPT-09` | Interrupts herself constantly, mid-thought, correcting | L34-CLASS-07 body: her thinking outruns her sentences | No |
| 11 | `B39-L23-OWN-03` | Attached to exactly one object and indifferent to all others | L34-CLASS-02 person | No |
| 12 | `B39-L08-REFLEX-08` | Tells the truth immediately and inconveniently before considering cost | L34-CLASS-09 self-constructed | Yes |

**Contradiction and cost.** *Pair 1:* demands exact answers from adults (`L06-INFO-01`) and cannot bear to be questioned closely herself (`L27-SHAME-01`) — collides whenever a tutor examines her. *Pair 2:* practises with adult discipline before dawn (`L24-SESSION-04`) and is defeated by ordinary hunger by mid-morning (`L30-ANCHOR-03`) — collides on every long ceremonial day. *Cost:* `L36-COST-06`, an honesty that has already made a permanent enemy of her father's deputy.

**Layer Register excerpt.** `B39-L01-POSTURE-03` never fully seats, perches forward `[DERIVED]` (from: L29-TOLER-02; age band) · `B39-L07-SELF-04` uses her tutor's voice for self-correction `[ASKED]` · `B39-L11-PLAY-10` N-A: is herself a child `[N-A]` · `B39-L17-STAGE-01` age-gated `[N-A]` · `B39-L25-KILL-01` has not killed `[ASKED]`.

**Usual drift entries.** First kill → no drift entry for `L01-HANDS-06`, which is immutable and does not move; the counting continues and one number is never said aloud (a rendering note, not a drift). First defeat → `L06-INFO-01` becomes `-02` observes rather than asks, Provisional, reverts when an adult answers her honestly. First PIS-5 → `L30-ANCHOR-04` retires, Permanent. Next realm crossing → `L26-DIRECTION-09` more impatient with the pace of her own progress, Permanent. Band crossing YC-1→YC-2 → `L10-INTERRUPT-09` becomes `-06`, Permanent; `L30-ANCHOR-07` retained (`L30-RETAIN-01`). Loss of tutor → `L24-SESSION-04` becomes `-07` will not practise where she can be seen, Permanent.

**Read-By-Others.** Noticed first: that she is already looking at you (`L33-FIRST-04`). Misread: her courtesy read as warmth; it is distance (`L33-MISREAD-04`). By rank: superiors overrate her, subordinates do not (`-05`). Intimates: her tutor knows the counting means fear.

---

## A.2 — Archetype: The Sovereign

**Example character:** Queen Aderin Sault, fifty-one, twenty-two years reigning.

**Signature Set (12), abbreviated:** `L15-ROOM-01` holds a room by stillness (origin L34-CLASS-01, the regency council she survived at nineteen; **immutable**) · `L01-OBJ-01` sets everything down two-handed and level (L34-CLASS-03, absorbed from her predecessor) · `L15-RECV-05` asks for the worst item first and refuses the preamble (L34-CLASS-09, self-constructed after a concealed famine report) · `L13-MASK-05` the mask is the office and goes on with the robe (L34-CLASS-01) · `L20-SEAL-03` reads twice and signs once (L34-CLASS-01) · `L08-POWERLESS-05` learns and uses names, always, and expects others to (L34-CLASS-02, her mother) · `L10-PRAISE-01` never praises directly; praise reaches its subject through a third party (L34-CLASS-01) · `L29-WAIT-04` takes a formal held position and maintains it exactly (L34-CLASS-01) · `L03-LATENCY-02` a held beat before every response in which the expression is chosen (L34-CLASS-01) · `L23-SLEEP-07` cannot sleep until a specific check is complete (L34-CLASS-05, the night of the succession) · `L18-WEIGHT-08` binding on the office and not the person, and she distinguishes carefully (L34-CLASS-09) · `L06-DILEM-06` chooses the reversible option, always, even when it is weaker (L34-CLASS-09; **immutable**).

**Contradiction and cost.** *Pair 1:* learns and uses every servant's name (`L08-POWERLESS-05`) and cannot recall the names of her own council's spouses — she attends downward and not sideways. *Pair 2:* reads twice and signs once (`L20-SEAL-03`) and has twice signed unread on the word of one specific person. *Cost:* `L36-COST-01`, a refusal to elevate a competent kinsman, which has cost her a faction.

**Sample drift entries (abbreviated).** First significant defeat → `L15-DEGRADE-05` becomes ceremonial; falls back on protocol, Provisional. First PIS-5 → `L03-LATENCY-02` lengthens to `-08`, Permanent. Loss of her Chancellor → `L10-PRAISE-01` becomes `-02` praises publicly and specifically, Permanent, *because the third party who carried her praise is gone*. Realm crossing → `L26-DIRECTION-05` more responsible, mapped. Age band → late adult → elder, `L15-DELEG-01` delegates completely, Permanent.

**Read-By-Others.** Misread: her stillness read as judgment; it is the held beat of `L03-LATENCY-02` (`L33-MISREAD-01`). By rank: superiors none; peers read her accurately; subordinates read her as cold; outsiders read her as formidable and warm, which nobody who works for her recognizes (`L33-BYRANK-08`).

---

## A.3 — Archetype: The Career Soldier

**Example character:** Sergeant Bel Worran — thirty-four, nineteen years' service, frontier rotations.

**Signature Set (12), abbreviated:** `L01-POSTURE-11` back to a wall and the room in front, in every room, without appearing to choose it (L34-CLASS-06 sustained conditions, frontier rotation; **immutable**) · `L02-FIRST-01` exits and how many people stand between them (L34-CLASS-06) · `L29-BEFORE-06` attends to the others; checks their gear before their own (L34-CLASS-01) · `L25-RETREAT-04` covers the retreat personally and leaves last (L34-CLASS-05 event, the Sapphire ford) · `L05-FOOD-01` fast, standing, finished before others have sat (L34-CLASS-01) · `L22-SLEEP-03` sleeps with a tool in reach and checks it before lying down (L34-CLASS-06) · `L13-DOWN-05` knows subordinates' circumstances and acts on it (L34-CLASS-02, a sergeant who did the same for them) · `L10-OATH-03` swears by the trade: gear, weather, and the failures peculiar to it (L34-CLASS-01) · `L03-CHANNEL-01` feeling goes into the hands, which find work (L34-CLASS-06) · `L25-PAIN-01` silent, always (L34-CLASS-01) · `L28-LOSE-01` goes immediately to practice and drills the specific failure (L34-CLASS-01) · `L36-COST-03` loyalty to a soldier the service has written off (L34-CLASS-05; **immutable**).

**Contradiction and cost.** *Pair 1:* checks everyone else's gear before their own (`L29-BEFORE-06`) and has twice deployed with a fault in their own kit. *Pair 2:* silent under pain (`L25-PAIN-01`) and complains continuously about food, weather and paperwork. *Cost:* the written-off soldier, which has cost them a promotion twice.

**Sample drift entries (abbreviated).** First failure to protect → `L13-DOWN-05` becomes `-06` maintains distance deliberately, believing familiarity costs lives, Permanent. First mercy received → `L25-MERCY-10` the debt governs how they handle it now, Permanent. PIS-5 → `L03-CHANNEL-01` fails; the hands stop finding work, Provisional, reverts on return to a working unit. Tier crossing → `L26-DIRECTION-07` unchanged in conduct, deliberately, mapped as *no change*, which is itself the registration.

---

## A.4 — Archetype: The Clerk of Record

**Example character:** Under-Clerk Vessa Molt, twenty-eight, eleven years at the same desk.

**Signature Set (12), abbreviated:** `L01-OBJ-02` squares each object to the edge of the surface, in any house (L34-CLASS-04 trade necessity; **immutable**) · `L02-FIRST-06` registers the paper first: what is written, sealed, filed, or conspicuously absent (L34-CLASS-04) · `L06-RULE-08` needs the rule written before acting, and will wait for it (L34-CLASS-01) · `L10-TURN-03` speaks only when addressed directly, and this holds under provocation (L34-CLASS-01) · `L29-HANDS-05` writing: notes, lists, a private record (L34-CLASS-04) · `L20-SEAL-01` reads the document fully before signing, however long the room waits (L34-CLASS-01) · `L09-CAPACITY-05` lies only by omission and holds that this is not lying (L34-CLASS-09) · `L12-NOTRADE-02` will not agree to alter, omit or destroy a document (L34-CLASS-09; **immutable**) · `L13-UP-03` reports problems immediately, including their own, unsoftened (L34-CLASS-01) · `L23-ROUTINE-01` a fixed daily sequence, unvaried for eleven years (L34-CLASS-04) · `L03-LEAK-04` the handwriting shows the feeling before the face does (L34-CLASS-04) · `L28-AMBIG-05` converts ambiguity to a procedural question: what does the record say (L34-CLASS-04).

**Contradiction and cost.** *Pair 1:* will not alter a document (`L12-NOTRADE-02`) and lies by omission daily (`L09-CAPACITY-05`) — collides the first time an omission is asked for in writing. *Pair 2:* speaks only when addressed (`L10-TURN-03`) and reports every problem immediately (`L13-UP-03`) — collides when the problem is in a room she has not been invited to. *Cost:* `L36-COST-02`, a thoroughness that makes her slow and is resented.

**Sample drift entries (abbreviated).** First betrayal → `L09-CAPACITY-05` becomes `-01` cannot lie outright, Permanent. Being ordered to alter a record → an `L08-LINE` test; unmapped, ruled at Check #21. PIS-5 → `L23-ROUTINE-01` becomes `-08` intensifies, rigid, Provisional. Promotion → `L10-TURN-03` becomes `-01`, Permanent.

---

## A.5 — Archetype: The Artificer

**Example character:** Journeyman Aur Tallow, forty, workshop-trained, no formal rank.

**Signature Set (12), abbreviated:** `L01-OBJ-04` tests weight, edge or seam of any unfamiliar object before doing anything else with it (L34-CLASS-04; **immutable**) · `L02-DEFORM-01` reads everything as a mechanism, people included (L34-CLASS-04) · `L24-TECH-05` dismantles new things immediately (L34-CLASS-04) · `L29-HANDS-02` maintenance: cleans, sharpens, oils, or checks something while waiting (L34-CLASS-04) · `L01-HANDS-11` carries a working residue they clean without noticing (L34-CLASS-04) · `L10-CRIT-08` criticizes by demonstrating the correct method without comment (L34-CLASS-02, their master) · `L23-SPACE-04` space arranged for work, with living accommodated in the margins (L34-CLASS-06) · `L06-ERROR-06` traces the error to its process and fixes the process (L34-CLASS-04) · `L12-NOTRADE-07` will not build or certify below a craft standard (L34-CLASS-01; **immutable**) · `L05-STRESS-04` makes tea as a way of buying time in a conversation (L34-CLASS-03, absorbed from their master) · `L20-PRIVATE-02` a fixed act before any dangerous undertaking (L34-CLASS-08 cultural) · `L11-MECH-07` trade humor, incomprehensible outside the workshop (L34-CLASS-04).

**Contradiction and cost.** *Pair 1:* reads people as mechanisms (`L02-DEFORM-01`) and is genuinely tender with apprentices (`L16-PHYS-01`). *Pair 2:* will not certify below standard (`L12-NOTRADE-07`) and has certified once, for a reason they will not discuss. *Cost:* `L36-COST-04`, a standard that loses them work.

**Sample drift entries (abbreviated).** Loss of their master → `L05-STRESS-04` becomes `-08` unchanged by stress, registered discipline, Permanent — *they stop making the tea*. First defeat (a failed commission) → `L06-ERROR-06` becomes `-09` records it privately, Permanent. PIS-5 → `L01-HANDS-11` intensifies to compulsive cleaning, Provisional.

---

## A.6 — Archetype: The Grandmaster

**Example character:** Grandmaster Oleth Vane, one hundred and nine, cultivation-arrested at sixty in appearance.

**Signature Set (12), abbreviated:** `L01-STILL-01` absolute stillness is the resting state; movement is a decision (L34-CLASS-11 cultivation practice; **immutable**) · `L06-HORIZON-10` cultivation-scale horizon; thinks in realms and decades, which distorts their handling of the immediate (L34-CLASS-11) · `L16-TEACH-04` teaches entirely by question and never states a conclusion (L34-CLASS-02) · `L26-WEAKER-10` conceals their level so the differential does not distort the exchange (L34-CLASS-09) · `L24-SENSE-06` announces the use of soul-sense, always, as a courtesy (L34-CLASS-01) · `L10-SILENCE-10` silence extended as a courtesy, to let another reach a conclusion unaided (L34-CLASS-01) · `L05-FOOD-10` eats little and irregularly; a tray sent up comes back untouched (L34-CLASS-11) · `L04-AGING-08` has begun giving things away, one object at a time, to chosen people (L34-CLASS-05) · `L20-DEAD-06` records the dead: names, dates, circumstances, and keeps the record (L34-CLASS-05) · `L07-COUNT-07` counts the dead, by name, and the list only grows (L34-CLASS-05) · `L26-FEAR-09` fear of outliving people (L34-CLASS-11; **immutable**) · `L23-CHILD-03` is transformed by children; the whole register changes (L34-CLASS-05).

**Contradiction and cost.** *Pair 1:* teaches only by question (`L16-TEACH-04`) and has twice given a direct order that saved a student's life and cost the student's respect. *Pair 2:* conceals their level as a courtesy (`L26-WEAKER-10`) and is quietly wounded when they are underestimated. *Cost:* `L36-COST-08`, a grudge maintained past all utility against a peer who is now dead.

**Layer Register line.** `B39-L30-BAND-08` chronologically elder, physically arrested by cultivation `[DERIVED]` (from: the cultivation arrest at sixty; Part 3 lifespan interface). It is a status that can be stated about the Grandmaster and not shown in one beat, so it is registered here and is not a Signature (Rule 39.3.3.3).

**Sample drift entries (abbreviated).** Death of a student → `L16-TEACH-04` becomes `-01` drill; repetition to reflex, Permanent — *the questions stop*. PIS-5 → `L01-STILL-01` holds (immutable); `L10-SILENCE-10` becomes `-06` silence as refusal, Provisional. Power loss → `L26-LOSS-09` turns to teaching and record-keeping, mapped. Realm crossing → `L26-DIRECTION-04` more withdrawn, mapped.

## A.7 — Instant Characters: Four Codes

**These four codes are non-canon.** They describe invented people who exist nowhere in the Starward Saga and show only the form of a Tier D code (Section 3.5). Every conduct code is from an open Layer and exists in the catalog; every value sits inside the limits of Rule 39.3.5.5. Each code is one line.

**A stable-hand who speaks two lines** (young adult; `PSM-2` is fragment tolerance and `PSM-8` the pause and trail-off profile; the task-first habit is the marked Signature):
`ROLE-STABLEHAND, YC-3, F3, PSM-2, PSM-8, B39-L01-STILL-04, B39-L10-FILLER-04[SIG], B39-L13-UP-07, B39-L23-ANIMAL-01, RLM-UNAWAKENED[AI], CC-S1[AI], AFN-NONE[AI], SOUL-NONE[AI], HEART-NONE[AI], LBI[AI], IS-1[AI], PIS-1[AI], CPT-OPEN`

**A court usher** (late adult; `PSM-3` is subordination depth; protocol conduct is not picked because L20 is barred; the waiting habit is the Signature):
`ROLE-USHER, B39-L30-BAND-06, F2, PSM-3, PSM-8, B39-L01-POSTURE-01, B39-L13-UP-01, B39-L21-ASSEMBLY-05, B39-L29-QUEUE-01[SIG], RLM-UNAWAKENED[AI], CC-S1[AI], AFN-NONE[AI], SOUL-NONE[AI], HEART-NONE[AI], OMI[AI], IS-1[AI], PIS-1[AI], CPT-OPEN`

**A guild clerk** (established adult; `PSM-6` is epigram appetite and `PSM-7` the technical-register default):
`ROLE-CLERK, B39-L30-BAND-05, F3, PSM-6, PSM-7, B39-L06-INFO-03, B39-L10-TURN-04, B39-L12-CLOSE-01[SIG], B39-L29-HANDS-05, RLM-UNAWAKENED[AI], CC-S1[AI], AFN-NONE[AI], SOUL-NONE[AI], HEART-NONE[AI], TRI[AI], IS-1[AI], PIS-2[AI], CPT-OPEN`

**An errand-runner** (an adolescent, `YC-2`; `PSM-1` is sentence-length distribution and `PSM-4` the question habit; the three `L30-ANCHOR` codes stand last among the conduct codes and are counted apart from the three to six; the cutting-across habit is the marked Signature and sits on an ordinary conduct code, not on an anchor):
`ROLE-RUNNER, YC-2, F3, PSM-1, PSM-4, B39-L01-GAIT-04[SIG], B39-L05-FOOD-01, B39-L13-STRANGER-09, B39-L21-STREET-02, B39-L30-ANCHOR-01, B39-L30-ANCHOR-03, B39-L30-ANCHOR-08, RLM-UNAWAKENED[AI], CC-S1[AI], AFN-NONE[AI], SOUL-NONE[AI], HEART-NONE[AI], ADI[AI], IS-1[AI], PIS-1[AI], CPT-OPEN`

The code holds 21 tokens: fourteen segments, with two `PSM` tokens, four conduct codes and three anchors.
